# The case against approving ACD-1.0

Read this before the arguments in favour. It is the complete set of facts that argue against
approval, written by the submitting side, and it is not balanced: nothing here is paired with a
reassurance in the same breath. Where an answer exists it is given afterwards and marked as such.
Where none exists, the row says so.

**Standard used to build this list.** A fact belongs here if a reviewer could reasonably raise
it, not if we think we can answer it. Two of the entries below have no answer, and one of them
is, on its own, sufficient reason to decline. That is the point of writing the list this way:
if every adverse fact came with a tidy rebuttal, the document would be advocacy wearing the
costume of candour.

## The list

### 状態の記法 —— この register は記録ではなく、潰すための backlog である

**すべての entry は `Status` 欄の先頭に、機械可読な state token を 1 つだけ持つ**（Check 474 が強制）。
**目的は「全部潰す」ことであり、途中で増減するのは問題ではない**（steward・2026-09-23）。

| token | 意味 | 潰し方 |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| **`[CLOSED]`** | もう残っているものが無い | —— |
| **`[TEXT]`** | **本文の変更で閉じる。** 1.0 は凍結なので `ACD-1.2-DRAFT.txt` で | errata へ起こし、草案へ反映する |
| **`[DOSSIER]`** | 我々の文書・Check の修正で閉じる | 直して Check で固定する |
| **`[EXTERNAL]`** | 外部（OSI の判断・弁護士・採用者）に依存する | **我々にできるのは条件を整えることだけ。何ができるかを entry に書く** |
| **`[TRADE]`** | 意図した設計上の取引。直すものは無い | **立場を述べ切れているかが合否。費用は消えない** |
| **`[OPEN]`** | **開いているが、どの経路で閉じるか未定** | 経路を決めて `TEXT` / `DOSSIER` / `EXTERNAL` へ移す |
| **`[UNTRIAGED]`** | **まだ読んで分類していない** | **読む。これ自体が backlog である** |

**⚠ 初回の付与（2026-09-23）は、既存の散文からの保守的な導出である。**
**140 種の判定語が使われており、機械では確信を持って分けられなかった 79 件を
`[UNTRIAGED]` としてある** ——**「分類した」と偽るより、未分類の数を見せるほうが潰せる。**

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **No lawyer has read the licence.** It has had no legal review of any kind | **[EXTERNAL]** **Unanswerable by us.** Only counsel can cure it, and none has been engaged **潰し方**: **弁護士を入れる以外に無い。** 我々にできるのは **(a) 不在を最初に自分から述べること**（提出パケット §5）、**(b) 弁護士の代替にならないと断ったうえで機械的に確かめられる性質を示すこと**（§4c）、**(c) 提出そのものが pro bono の弁護士の目を通す経路であると理解して出すこと**（`review-precedents.md` §1.52）。**代替だと主張しないこと**が規律である。 |
| 2 | **The decisive step in the closest precedent is unavailable here.** The Unlicense was approved after **lawyers, "both US and non-US"**, concluded the document "would most likely be interpreted as a license and that the license met the OSD" (License Review Committee recommendation, read at source 2026-09-06). **It was not one opinion but several, across jurisdictions.** That reading is what resolved the dedication-versus-licence question there. Nothing equivalent exists for ACD-1.0 | **[EXTERNAL]** **Unanswerable by us.** Same root as #1  **Discoverability is the only honest lever on this, and it was not pulled until 2026-09-09.** ACD-1.0 §16.3 says the instrument may be applied by anyone to any work, and `LICENSE` repeats it — *"offered for general use by anyone: ACD-1.0 contains nothing specific to this project, this person, or this jurisdiction"*. **Neither statement was on `README.md`**, which is the first surface a visitor to the repository reads; the licence appeared there only as a badge and as a callout addressed to reviewers arriving from the OSI thread. **This is #85 one surface further out** — that entry recorded telling machines about the licence and not humans. **Adoption cannot be manufactured and must not be**, but a true statement that is not where readers are is not an offer. A line was added pointing at the text, at §16.3's no-project-specifics claim, and — in the same sentence — at **the adverse list and the absence of legal review**, so that the invitation and its warnings arrive together **潰し方**: **採用は AI には作れない**（採用の捏造は失格事由）。**我々にできるのは discoverability だけ** ——§16.3 が「誰でも自分の著作物に使える」と述べ、README と入口ページがそれを最も読まれる面で言う。**2026-09-09 に初めてそれを実行した。** |
| 3 | **A public-domain dedication, taken alone, is not approvable.** The escape is §4, a licence granted independently of §3 (§4.4). That construction has never been tested by any court in any jurisdiction, and no court has characterised the instrument as a whole | **[EXTERNAL]** **Unresolved.** The structure is argued in submission §1b; whether it functions is a legal question nobody has answered **潰し方**: **無い。** §4.4 の構造は提出文 §1b で論じてあるが、**機能するかどうかは法廷か弁護士しか答えられない。** 我々にできるのは、**受領者が §3 の有効性を判断しなくてよい形にしてある**ことを述べ切ることだけで、それは実行済み（§4.4 は E28 で肯定形へ）。 |
| 4 | **One adopter: this repository.** No independent party has chosen it | **[CLOSED]** **Answered narrowly, and the venue matters (added 2026-09-06 after reading both sources).** Adoption is a **listed criterion for SPDX** — "substantial use … in many projects, or in one or a few significant projects", or for a new licence "definitive plans for use in significant projects" (#78) — and by that criterion this fails, which is why SPDX is not being approached. **It is not a stated gate for `license-review`**: the contemporaneous MG-BY-2.0 submission (2025-02) declared "Approved or Used by Projects: **No**" and was reviewed on the merits through three rounds. **That does not make one adopter costless** — a reviewer may still weigh it, and #7 records that the closest precedent was placed narrowly partly on such grounds. What it does mean is that the honest statement is "this fails SPDX's criterion and is information, not a bar, at OSI", rather than treating it as disqualifying everywhere.  Reusability is shown structurally (submission §4b); adoption is not claimed and does not follow |
| 5 | **The goal was the owner's; the concrete legal design and the text are an AI's.** He decided to have a licence of his own and said it should aim at external approval; **he did not write a legal specification, did not supply a list of legal requirements, and did not write any clause.** An AI agent generated the concrete legal design and the text and developed it further under his standing delegation. **The name ACD-1.0, the generalised instrument and the submission material took shape during that delegated work, and he came to know their concrete state afterwards** — **corrected 2026-09-23 (#216); this entry previously said he did not commission it at all and learned of the licence's existence afterwards, which the steward's own letter of 2026-09-14 contradicts.** He read the full text and understood it before sending | **[EXTERNAL]** **Disclosed, not defended.** Provenance does not make a text good or bad, but reviewers are entitled to weigh it and this submission does not ask them not to **潰し方**: 我々にできるのは開示を正確に保つことだけで、**それは #216〜#219 で完了している。来歴をどう重み付けるかは OSI の判断であり、B14 がその生きた問いである。** |
| 6 | **It is long.** 16 sections, 82 clauses, 597 lines, for an instrument whose stated purpose is to reserve nothing — **and the length has a second dimension recorded 2026-09-06: how it is likely to be carried onto the list.** The list's code of conduct asks participants to be "concise and low-volume", and that was restated in the August 2026 review. A submitter with 82 clauses, twenty-odd dossier documents and an 800-line packet is under an obvious temptation to answer at length | **[TRADE]** **Accepted cost, and as of 2026-09-09 an argued one rather than an asserted one.** The trade is argued in review-responses-meta Q17. The opposite criticism sank nothing — the Unlicense was approved while broadly agreed to be poorly drafted — so length is not obviously the safer error. **Two things were added on 2026-09-09.** **First, the objection now has a named source and a rationale attached**: Pamela Chestek, chair of the Licensing Committee, wrote on `license-discuss` on 2026-06-23 of another submission — *"It is now 3888 words, longer than the GPLv3. **It is excessively wordy and proscriptive. These qualities mean that no one other than you will ever use this license, so it does not seem to be a good candidate for approval.**"* — and named the addition of **a moral-rights clause** as an instance of "mission creep", which is the very clause `review-precedents.md` §1.59 records as filling a gap the incumbents leave. **The same section reads as differentiation from one side and as bloat from the other.** **Second, we finally measured.** Until then this entry described the licence as "16 sections, 82 clauses, 597 lines" — **not the unit a reviewer uses.** Measured 2026-09-09: **ACD-1.0 is 4,896 words in full and 4,574 in its operative text; GPL-3.0 is 5,644 and 4,617.** ACD-1.0 is **43 words shorter than GPLv3's operative text and 748 shorter overall** — it is not, on any measure we can make, a long licence by the benchmark the chair chose. **That answers the number and not the argument.** Her inference was *wordy ∧ proscriptive → no one else will use it*; ACD-1.0 attaches no conditions at all (§10.1, §4.3) so the second premise does not hold, **but the conclusion is independently available against us, because we have one adopter (#2).** Answering the half we can measure and leaving the half we cannot would be taking the convenient half **合否**: **長さそのものは消せない**（gap 部を削れば B10 が悪化し、機構部の圧縮では委員長が engage しなかった 3,888 語に届かない —— register B3 が実測してある）。**したがって合否は「短い入口が実際に短いか」**で、入口ページの到達距離 30% → 8%、≈6,250 語の短い道がその答えである（B13）。**⚠ 費用は増え続ける** ——E31 / E32 で +72 語。**足すたびに register へ書く。** |
| 7 | **Realistic best case is a narrow placement.** The Unlicense was put in a special-purpose category over the submitter's objection, expressly because of its dedication nature | **[TRADE]** **Accepted.** Submission §4a disclaims any request for a broader designation **合否**: **狭い分類を受け入れると自分から述べているか。** 提出文 §4a が広い designation を求めないと明記している。**⚠ 逆側**: 狭い分類は B2（採用）をさらに難しくする ——**狭い枠に入ったものは、その枠を探している人にしか見つからない。** |
| 8 | **Jurisdictional effectiveness is unverified everywhere.** §3 fallback, §8.4 patent reach, §12 moral-rights construction, §13/§14 against consumer-protection law — none tested | **[EXTERNAL]** **Unresolved by design.** `ACD-1.0.jurisdictions.md` records these as questions and refuses to state conclusions **潰し方**: **法域ごとの有効性は、法廷か弁護士でしか確かめられない。** `jurisdictions.md` は問いとして記録し**結論を述べない**姿勢を保っており、それが我々の側でできる全部である。**B1（法的レビュー不在）と同じ根。** |
| 9 | **§5.2 contains covenants by the Dedicator.** A reviewer may read a covenant as an obligation that makes the instrument less than fully permissive | **[CLOSED]** **Answered.** §5.2 binds only the Dedicator; §10.1 states that no condition is imposed on You. Argued in review-responses-boilerplate Q26 |
| 10 | **The steward is one individual.** No organisation maintains it, and there is no succession plan for the licence itself | **[CLOSED]** **Answered partially.** review-responses-meta Q12. The absence of an or-later mechanism (Q33) is a deliberate consequence: a lone steward should not hold a blank cheque over future versions |
| 11 | **No versioning mechanism.** "ACD-1.0 or later" cannot be written | **[TRADE]** **Accepted.** Same as Apache-2.0, MIT and BSD; explained in Q33 **合否**: **「無いこと」が設計だと述べ切れているか。** Q33 が理由（単独 steward に将来版の白紙委任を与えない）を述べ、Apache-2.0 / MIT / BSD が同じく持たないことを示している。**⚠ 逆側**: or-later が無いと、欠陥が見つかった版に留まる採用者を救う経路も無い ——**PHP の retire（#209）は自ライセンスの clause 5 で移行できたが、我々にはそれが無い。** |
| 12 | **Not registered with SPDX.** Scanners will report `LicenseRef-` or `NOASSERTION` | **[TRADE]** **Accepted.** A registration-state problem, not a text problem; the order (discussion → review → SPDX) is deliberate **合否**: **登録状態の問題であってテキストの問題ではない、と述べ切れているか。** 順序（discussion → review → SPDX）は意図的で、`REUSE.toml` は適合形（`LicenseRef-ACD-1.0`）を file 水準でも宣言している。**⚠ 逆側**: scanner が `NOASSERTION` を返す間、**§6.5 が述べる「自動化システムが判定できる許諾」から最も遠い。** |
| 13 | **Nothing has actually started yet.** The message is on `license-discuss`, which is not the approval venue. No submission to `license-review` exists | **[EXTERNAL]** **Stated, not spun.** This document exists before the process does **潰し方**: `license-review` への提出で閉じる。**提出は B14 の下で止まっており、可否は steward と OSI の側にある。** |
| 14 | **The licence has never been tested by a dispute.** No court, no arbitration, no adversarial reading | **[EXTERNAL]** **Unresolved.** True of most licences at submission time, which is a mitigation and not an answer **潰し方**: **紛争が起きるまで閉じない。** 提出時点でほとんどのライセンスが同じ状態にあることは緩和であって答えではない、と entry 自身が述べている。 |

## Clause-level adversarial re-reading (added 2026-08-28)

Five clauses were put to a hostile reading at the suggestion of a third party. The results are
placed here rather than in the favourable material, because three of the five are costs and one
is a fair criticism of the text itself.

| # | Adverse reading | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 15 | **§2.6 makes submission itself the act of dedicating, by default.** A Contribution is under this Dedication and its author becomes a Dedicator "unless they state otherwise at the time of submission." A licence cannot by its own words determine the legal effect of a third party's act: whether a contributor is bound depends on notice and assent, not on this clause saying so. It is also an **opt-out by silence**, which sits awkwardly beside §6.2, where the instrument refuses to read silence as a reservation | **[TRADE]** **Real tension, partly answered.** §2.7 keeps the reach honest — nothing is dedicated that the person did not hold, and §2.6 cannot manufacture assent. In practice it operates as a stated default that a project must put contributors on notice of, not as a self-executing transfer. The dossier previously described contributions in a way that omitted this default; that description has been corrected **検証 2026-09-23**: §2.6 は E22 / E16 で 2 度直したが、**「ライセンスが第三者の行為の効果を自分で決められない」という緊張は条文に残る。** **潰し方は 2 つしかない —— §2.6 の既定を落とすか、既定であることを本文で明示するか。どちらも 1.2 草案の設計判断である。** **条文でできることは尽きている。** §2.6 は既定であることを明示し（*"unless they state otherwise at the time of submission"*）、§2.7 が到達範囲を縛り、E16 で識別の約束が 2 経路とも塞がれた。**残るのは「ライセンスは第三者の行為の法的効果を自分で決められない」という、どの instrument にも共通の限界**である。**合否は「自己執行ではなく既定であると述べ切れているか」。** |
| 16 | **§8.4's patent grant is very broad.** It reaches "every patent claim owned or controlled by the Dedicator, now or in future" that would be infringed by Computational Use **or by any model, parameter set, weight, embedding, or output resulting from that use.** An output can be far from the Work, so a claim in an unrelated field could be swept in. It is materially broader than Apache-2.0, which limits the grant to claims necessarily infringed by the contribution | **[TRADE]** **Accepted cost, with a consequence worth naming.** The breadth runs against the Dedicator, not against You, so it is not an OSD problem. But counsel for a patent-holding organisation would likely refuse it, which means **the instrument is expensive for exactly the adopters whose adoption would carry weight**. That is part of why adoption is one repository **合否**: **広さが Dedicator に向いていること（受領者に向いていないこと）を述べ切れているか。** OSD 問題ではないが、**特許を持つ組織の法務は #17 と併せて読む** ——**両方を 1 か所で読める形にしてあるかが実際の分かれ目**で、`objection-map.md` と提出文 §4 がそれをしている。 |
| 17 | **§5.2(b) is a wide covenant against collateral obligations.** The Dedicator promises not to impose, "by contract, terms of service, access condition, click-through, registration requirement, or any other collateral means," an obligation in respect of the Work. The boundary between "in respect of the Work" and "in respect of a service that uses it" is not drawn | **[TRADE]** **Accepted cost.** Again it binds only the Dedicator. But read together with #16, an organisation adopting ACD-1.0 gives up more than it may realise: arguably dual licensing, paid support terms touching the Work, and gated distribution. The instrument is demanding of its adopter by design, and adopters should be told so plainly rather than discovering it **合否**: **#16 と併せて読まれる形になっているか。** 単体では「Dedicator しか縛らない」で足りるが、**併せると、採用する組織が dual licensing・有償サポートの一部・アクセス制御を手放すことになる。** **その総量を先に自分から述べているかが分かれ目**で、提出文 §4 の `Cost:` 欄がそれを担う。 |
| 18 | **§16.1 instructs adopters to write an identifier that no registry has assigned.** The recommended notice contains `SPDX-License-Identifier: ACD-1.0`, but SPDX has not registered ACD-1.0. Today the conforming form for an unregistered licence is `LicenseRef-`, so a notice written exactly as §16.1 directs is not SPDX-conforming, and scanners will not match it | **[CLOSED]** **Fair criticism of the text. Not fixed.** The text is frozen for the duration of the discussion (Check 453 pins its digest), so this is **reported and left in place** rather than patched — changing it underneath a live review would make the review of it meaningless. It is a candidate correction for 1.1. Note the practical effect is limited: the identifier a licence assigns itself is conventionally the string SPDX later registers, and `ACD-1.0.faq.md` already tells adopters what to write while unregistered **検証 2026-09-23**: **草案で閉じている。** `ACD-1.2-DRAFT.txt` §16.1 の推奨 notice は `SPDX-License-Identifier: ACD-1.2` の次行に **`(LicenseRef-ACD-1.2 while not on the SPDX License List)`** を持ち、**この entry が求めた併記そのものである。** 1.0 は凍結のまま。 **⚠ 本日 1 度この entry を `TEXT`（未解決）と誤判定した** —— `grep -A10` で出力が §16.1 の notice の途中で切れており、**切れた表示から結論した**。**PR 1225（`#` を付けない —— この register では `#N` は不利な事実の採番である）の増分で記録した「`cut -c1-200` で切れた表示から書き写した」と同じ形であり、同じ増分の中で自分で見つけた。**（**⚠ この注記の初版は PR 番号を `#` 付きで書き、Check 460 が 2 度捕まえた —— **その class を説明している文の中で、2 度その違反をした。** 1 度目は「PR」を前置すれば通ると考えたが、検出器は前置語を見ていない。**検出器が何を見ているかを読まずに直した。**） **現物を読むときは、表示が途中で切れていないかを先に確かめる。** |
| 19 | **§15.1 tells the reader how to construe the instrument**, and §5.2(c) has the Dedicator covenant not to argue for a narrower reading. A party cannot instruct a court | **[CLOSED]** **Answered, with one addition.** The general point is argued at review-responses-boilerplate Q22: a construction rule does not bind a court but is evidence of intent, which is all it claims to be. The addition the hostile reading surfaces is the **interaction with #16** — where §8.4's scope is ambiguous, §15.1 resolves the ambiguity *against* the Dedicator, compounding the breadth rather than limiting it |

| 20 | **§16.3 invites anyone to apply the notice to any work** "without permission from, notice to, or any relationship with its authors." §1.3 and §2.7 keep the *effect* honest — nothing is given that the applier does not hold — but the harm a reviewer will point at is not to the true owner. It is to the **downstream recipient**, who sees a machine-readable notice this very instrument invited anyone to emit, and whose reliance §13.2 then disclaims. An instrument whose stated purpose includes reliable machine-readable signalling weakens that signal in its own §16 | **[CLOSED]** **Fair criticism; wording broader than needed.** The clause is doing necessary work — it is the textual basis for the reusability claim in submission §4b, so it cannot simply be narrowed without weakening that. A tighter form ("by anyone, to any work in which they hold rights") would keep the reusability point and drop the invitation. **Reported, not fixed:** the text is frozen. 1.1 candidate **検証 2026-09-23**: **草案で閉じている。** `ACD-1.2-DRAFT.txt` §16.3 は *"by anyone, to any work **in which they hold rights**"* となっており、**この entry が提案した狭い形そのものである。** 1.0 は凍結のまま。 |
| 21 | **§2.9 declares its own insolvency-law classification** ("and so is not executory"), and states that no proceeding "affects a permission granted here." A document cannot determine how a forum's insolvency law classifies it; the Countryman-style tests belong to the forum. The mechanism is circular under hostile reading: §2.9 binds an officer by deeming them a §2.8 transferee, but whether that deeming is effective is the same question insolvency law decides | **[CLOSED]** **Overstated in form; sound in substance.** The clause does give the facts before the conclusion — no continuing obligation on either side — and those facts are what any executory-contract test actually examines. Stating them is the strongest thing a text can do. Asserting the outcome is not, and this text asserts it **検証 2026-09-23**: **草案で閉じている。** *"and so is not executory"* は errata E5 で削除され、**現行草案の条文には 0 件**（残る 1 件はヘッダで削除を説明している散文）。E30 が第 1 文の断定も文書側の約束へ寄せた。1.0 は凍結のまま。 |
| 22 | **§6.4 says outputs are not "encumbered by ... any Covered Right of the Dedicator" in absolute form.** Where an output substantially reproduces the Work, copyright in the Work plainly subsists in that reproduction, so the sentence appears to conflate "no right exists" with "rights exist and are unconditionally given away" | **[CLOSED]** **Answered, with a wording caveat.** §6.5 says §6 "states expressly what Sections 3 to 5 would in any event" do — §6 is declaratory, so "encumbered" means "burdened by an enforceable claim," and there is none, because §4.1 grants unconditionally. The operative result is right. The phrasing is loose enough that a reviewer can press it, and "no enforceable claim arises" would have been the precise form. 1.1 candidate |
| 23 | **§15.7 states an intention about conflict of laws without resolving it.** "It is intended to operate under the law of each jurisdiction in which the Work is used, according to that law" reads as depeçage by declaration, which parties cannot dictate — a forum applies its own conflict rules | **[CLOSED]** **Answered in part (review-responses-clauses, §15.7 entry, addresses the choice-of-law silence). One addition from this reading:** the absence of a governing law interacts with #3. Whether §3 is effective, and how the instrument is characterised, are both forum-dependent — so the questions §4.4 relieves the *recipient* of are not thereby removed from the *analysis*, only from the recipient's burden |
| 24 | **🔴 The submitting side misdescribed its own text.** Until 2026-08-28 this dossier and the submission packet said "§3's fallback licence." §3 is the surrender and waiver; the licence is §4, and §4.4 states it "is granted independently of Section 3 and does not depend on Section 3 being ineffective." The error made our own case weaker and wrong — a fallback invites "who decides, under which law, and when," while an independent grant does not | **[CLOSED]** **Corrected, and recorded here rather than quietly.** `ACD-1.0.review-responses.md` had it right all along (it quotes §4.4); the newer documents contradicted it. A reviewer who caught this would have been entitled to discount everything else we assert about the text |

| 25 | **§15.4's reformation mechanism may be unavailable in the jurisdiction that needs it most.** §15.4 directs that an invalid provision "is to be reformed to the minimum extent necessary to make it valid and enforceable there." German law generally does not permit *geltungserhaltende Reduktion* — reduction preserving validity — for standard terms: an invalid clause falls away rather than being trimmed to the permissible maximum. Germany is the jurisdiction this dossier names as the leading example of a system that rejects outright surrender of authorial rights, so the device meant to rescue §3 there is one that forum may decline to apply | **[CLOSED]** **Answered structurally; a residual question remains.** Two things blunt it. First, whether a gratuitous unilateral grant is "standard terms" at all is itself unsettled — there is no exchange and no counterparty imposing them. Second, and more decisively, §15.4's second sentence severs per-jurisdiction rather than globally, and §4.4 makes the licence independent of §3 — so if §3 is severed in Germany, **§4 is untouched and the recipient still holds a licence there**. The residual question is not whether permission survives (it does) but how a German court characterises what remains. Recorded because the first-order attack is real even though the structure absorbs it |
| 26 | **The text declines to warrant that it works.** §13.2 states in terms that the Dedicator does not warrant "THAT ANY SECTION OF THIS DEDICATION IS EFFECTIVE IN ANY JURISDICTION." A reviewer can quote that sentence on its own as the instrument conceding it may not operate anywhere | **[TRADE]** **Accepted and intentional.** The alternative is to warrant effectiveness, which no drafter can honestly do for an untested instrument and which would contradict everything else this dossier says about the limits of what has been verified (submission §4c). It is quoted here rather than left to be found, because the sentence is genuinely striking out of context and reviewers should meet it from us first **合否**: **保証しないことを述べ切れているか、そして代わりに何を示しているか。** 有効性を保証するのは未検証の instrument について誠実にできない。**代わりに示すのは機械的に確かめられる性質（§4c）で、弁護士の代替だと主張しないことが規律である**（#1 と同じ）。 |

| 27 | **The recommended notice says "no conditions are imposed," but §16.4 does impose one.** The licence text "may not be distributed in modified form under the name 'Autonomous Commons Dedication' or under the identifier 'ACD-1.0'." A reviewer reading the notice in §16.1 and then §16.4 three lines later has a fair prima facie contradiction to raise | **[CLOSED]** **Answered by the text, though not by the notice.** §10.1 is expressly limited: it imposes no condition "**in respect of the Work**." §16.4 governs the licence text, which is not the Work — the Work is whatever a Dedicator applies the instrument to. The two therefore do not overlap, and the restriction is the same name-integrity provision GPL and Apache-2.0 carry for their own texts, a kind of term OSD 4 contemplates for derived works of the *software* — **cited as context, not as the authority**, because §16.4 is not a term of the Work at all (§10.5, §16.6) and therefore does not need OSD 4's shelter; reading it as though it did would concede that §16.4 is a condition on derived works, which is the weaker of the two available answers (OSD text read at source 2026-09-06). What is imprecise is the **shorthand**: the notice drops the qualifier that makes the statement true. That is where a reviewer will aim, and it is a 1.1 candidate for the notice wording rather than for §10.1 |

## What the AI-native comparison added (2026-09-04)

Three instruments now before OSI — OpenMDW-1.1 (Linux Foundation) and ModelGo MG0-2.0 /
MG-BY-2.0 (NUS) — were read against ACD-1.0. Four adverse facts came out of it. The comparison
itself is in `ACD-1.0.comparison.md` §1.5.

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 28 | **ACD-1.0 is not the only AI-native instrument in front of OSI.** OpenMDW-1.1 and ModelGo v2 are in active review. Until now this dossier argued proliferation in the abstract — "no existing licence closes the gap." That framing was written without accounting for two instruments drafted for adjacent gaps, one of them by the Linux Foundation | **[CLOSED]** **Weakens the case, and the framing has been corrected.** The gap ACD-1.0 addresses (computational use of *any* work, including material whose authorship is uncertain) is still not the gap those two address (licensing *model artefacts*). But "no one else is working on this" was never true and is no longer claimed **潰し方は実行済み** —— *「誰も取り組んでいない」*という枠は撤回され、gap の主張は *computational use of any work* と *model artefacts* の差として述べ直されている。**proliferation の是非そのものは #84 / B10 が扱う。** |
| 29 | **ACD-1.0's most distinctive provisions sit where OSI's review capacity is thinnest.** A reviewer in the OpenMDW thread observed that the "OSI community has a limited history of accumulated discussion on intellectual property rights beyond copyright." ACD-1.0 leans on §7 (sui generis database rights), §8 (patents, reaching models and outputs) and §12 (moral rights) — three areas in exactly that category | **[EXTERNAL]** **Unresolved procedural risk.** It cuts both ways: the questions may not get expert scrutiny, or they may get scrutiny that no one is confident in. Neither is a good position for an instrument with no legal review of its own (#1) **潰し方**: **list の専門性の構成は我々が動かせない。** 両向き（精査されないか、誰も自信の無い精査になるか）を記録してあり、**どちらも法的レビュー不在（B1）と重なる**ことも書いてある。 |
| 30 | **The criticisms already levelled at OpenMDW apply to ACD-1.0 as well.** Its review raised that the licence "effectively forces due diligence for the model onto the user" and "has the potential to encourage openwashing." ACD-1.0 does the same through §13.2, which warrants neither that rights subsist, nor that the Dedicator holds them, nor that use infringes no one | **[TRADE]** **Accepted, and shared with the field.** Every instrument that disclaims title shifts diligence to the recipient. That does not make the criticism wrong — it makes it structural, and worth answering rather than deflecting to "everyone does it" **合否**: **構造的な批判であって我々固有の欠陥ではない、と述べたうえで、それでも受領者が負う diligence を隠していないか。** §2.7 と §13.2 が「持っている権利にしか届かない」「保証しない」を述べる。**⚠ 逆側**: 構造的であることは、受領者にとって軽いことを意味しない。 |
| 31 | **ACD-1.0 does not cover trade secret rights; OpenMDW does, expressly.** Where a model's protection rests on confidentiality rather than copyright, ACD-1.0's Covered Rights (§1.5 — copyright, performances, broadcast, database, unfair extraction, "any right of similar effect") arguably do not reach it | **[TRADE]** **Genuine coverage gap, contested in significance.** Whether licensing a trade secret has legal meaning at all was itself questioned in OpenMDW's review, so this is not simply "they cover more than us." But for the model case it is a difference in their favour, and ACD-1.0 makes no claim to it **意図した射程の外である。** §1.5 の Covered Rights は営業秘密を含まず、**含めるのは射程の拡大であって欠落の修復ではない。** **合否は「主張していないことを主張していないと述べ切れているか」**で、`comparison.md` がそれを述べている。 |

The submission packet should lead with that pair and treat the rest as supporting, not as equals in the argument **合否**: **比較の広さを次版で絞るかどうかは設計判断であり、誤りではない。** `comparison.md` §1 は MIT-0 と 0BSD を効果上の最近接として既に名指ししている。**⚠ 逆側**: 絞ると、**gap の主張を支える比較対象も減る** ——広い比較は proliferation への答えの一部でもある。 |

| 40 | **The known defect with the highest severity had no prepared answer for reviewers.** E1 — §16.1 directing adopters to write an unregistered SPDX identifier — was recorded in the errata and answered for *adopters* in the FAQ, but the anticipated-response documents, which are what a reviewer's objection would meet, said nothing about it. The one place in the text that tells a reader to do something incorrect was the one place with no prepared answer | **[CLOSED]** **Corrected.** A Q&A entry now states the defect in the first sentence, gives the severity as ours rather than waiting for it to be assigned, explains why it is not being fixed (the freeze protects the reviewer, not us), and bounds the practical effect. **The general lesson is that "how do we guide an adopter around this" and "how do we answer a reviewer who raises it" are different documents, and having the first does not produce the second** |

| 41 | **The warranty disclaimer is weaker than it looks, because nothing has to travel with the work.** Raised against the Unlicense in its 2020 review — "there's no requirement to include the license on redistribution, making me wonder how effective the warranty disclaimer is" — and it applies here with more force, because §10.2 states expressly that You need not retain the file or reproduce any notice. A disclaimer asserted against someone who never saw it is on worse footing, and in the United States conspicuousness is a live doctrine | **[TRADE]** **Accepted; the trade was made knowingly.** Requiring notice retention would be a condition, and §10.1 exists to have none. What makes the trade defensible is where the loss falls: **an unenforceable disclaimer exposes the Dedicator, not the recipient.** That is why this is disclosed rather than argued inside the OSD conformance section — it is a cost the giver bears, not a restriction on the user **合否**: **損失がどこに落ちるかを述べ切れているか。** notice 保持を求めれば条件になり §10.1 に反する。**落ちるのは受領者ではなく、来歴を追いたい第三者である。** **⚠ 逆側**: それは「誰も損をしない」ではない ——**origin-laundering に答えが無い**ことは提出文 §4 の `Cost:` に書いてある。 |
| 42 | **Precedent read properly cuts both ways, and the Unlicense review contains an argument we cannot use.** Its approval turned partly on the reading that its operative sentence functioned as a grant despite never using the words "grant" or "permission" — the same latitude BSD receives. ACD-1.0 does not need that latitude (§4.1 says "grants You a … licence"), so the precedent helps here. But the same review shows a reviewer maintaining that the instrument was "not a license" at all, and that position was not withdrawn — it was outvoted. **A submission of this shape can be textually clearer than its precedent and still meet the same objection** | **[TRADE]** **Unresolved by design.** No amount of drafting removes the question of whether a dedication-plus-grant is one instrument or two; §1b puts it first for that reason **どれだけ起草しても消えない問い**（dedication + grant が 1 つの instrument か 2 つか）であり、**だから提出文 §1b が最初に置いている。** **合否は、避けずに先頭へ置いているか。** Unlicense の承認理由のうち**我々が使えない論**（*grant* の語を使わずに grant として機能した）も同じ節に記録してある。 |

| 43 | **Four of the six distinguishing features are closable by amendment.** Express permission for training and TDM, a statement that no reservation is made, a patent grant reaching models and outputs, and database rights — each of these can be added to an existing licence as a clause. If the incumbent stewards move, the case for a separate instrument narrows from six features to two: not presuming that a right subsists, and imposing no condition at all | **[TRADE]** **Accepted, and stated before it is put to us.** `comparison.md` §1.35 separates what an amendment closes from what it does not, and says plainly that the two survivors carry the argument rather than the six. The claim is not that ACD-1.0 stays distinctive whatever happens — it is that these two differences follow from where a licence starts rather than from what it contains, and restructuring around a premise is not an amendment **合否**: **修正で閉じるものと閉じないものを、先に自分から分けているか。** `comparison.md` §1.35 がそれをしている。**⚠ 逆側**: 4 つが修正で閉じるということは、**既存ライセンスの保守者が同じことを 4 行でできる**ということでもある ——**gap の寿命は我々が決めない。** |

## Sections the adverse list had never examined (2026-09-04)

At the time of this pass the list stood at 43 entries. They were checked against where they came
from, and four sections of the text had produced none: §3, §7, §11 and §14. Two of those yielded something on a hostile reading.

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 44 | **§7 restates what §1.5 already covers, and a reviewer can ask what the extra section is doing.** §1.5 defines Covered Rights to include "sui generis database rights, rights against unfair extraction", so §7.1's statement that such a right "is a Covered Right and Sections 3 to 6 apply to it" adds nothing to the operative position. §7.2 goes further and permits extraction and re-utilisation "repeatedly and systematically, without limit and without condition" — language borrowed from the Database Directive, which invites the question of whether a licence should be drafting in the vocabulary of one region's statute | **[TRADE]** **Deliberate redundancy, and the same trade as §6.5.** The Directive's terms of art are what a European reader searches for; a permission phrased only in general terms leaves them checking whether "reproduce and distribute" reaches systematic extraction. The cost is a section that could be cut without changing what anyone may do — which is exactly the objection the length criticism (#6) makes, applied to a specific place **合否**: **冗長が読者のためであると述べ切れているか。** Directive の術語は欧州の読者が実際に検索する語である。**⚠ 逆側**: **#206(A) が記録した委員長の一般則「冗長な条項は無害ではない」が正面から当たる** ——鎖は *冗長 → 義務 → 破られる → 終了* で、**ACD には最後の鎖が無いが最初の鎖は残る。** |
| 45 | **§11.1 grants no trademark right, but §16.2 makes the name and identifier carry legal meaning.** *(Corrected 2026-09-04: this entry originally said the connecting sentence was missing from the text. It is not — §10.5 states that Section 16 "concerns the text of this Dedication as a document, and not the Work", and §16.6 repeats that §16.4 and §16.5 "are not terms of the Work, they bind no recipient of the Work." The entry stands only as a reading-order problem: §11.1 arrives before either.)* §16.2 provides that the identifier "ACD-1.0", an SPDX tag, or "a reference to this Dedication by name" is sufficient notice of the terms; §16.4 forbids applying that name to a modified text. So adopters are expected to use a name in which no right is granted, and the instrument regulates that use — while §11.1 says it grants nothing in "any trademark, service mark, trade name, logo, or personal name" | **[CLOSED]** **Coherent, but the coherence is not on the page.** Nominative use is preserved by §11.2, and naming a licence to say which licence applies is exactly that. §16.4's restriction runs to the licence text rather than to the Work (§10.1's limitation), so it is not a condition on You. What is missing is a sentence tying the three together; a reader who meets §11.1 first can reasonably think §16 is asking for something §11 withheld. **A candidate for 1.1, and a question worth answering before it is asked** **検証 2026-09-23**: **草案で閉じている**（errata E7）。`ACD-1.2-DRAFT.txt` §11.1 は *"Where Section 16 speaks of the name and the identifier … it asks nothing of You in respect of the Work (Section 10.5)"* を持ち、**読む順に依存しない。** 1.0 は凍結のまま。 |

| 46 | **The "no termination" design forecloses a question others must answer, and buys that with a real cost.** A licence granting over several kinds of right, but reacting to litigation over only some of them, has to explain the boundary — a question put to another submission in the September 2026 review, where the grant covered copyright, patent, trade secret and database rights while termination fired only on patent or copyright claims. ACD-1.0 cannot be asked this, because §10.4 terminates nothing "for any reason" and §8.2 says the absence of patent retaliation "is deliberate". **The cost is that a recipient who sues the Dedicator keeps every permission**, and defensive termination is widely regarded as a feature | **[TRADE]** **Accepted trade, now stated where a reviewer will look — and the trade has a second side, added 2026-09-06.** In the August 2026 OpenMDW review a participant argued that **defensive termination provisions may themselves be "restrictions" under OSD 9**, anchoring it in Apache-2.0's patent-termination clause having been treated as a source of GPLv2 incompatibility. If that reading holds, the feature this entry records as missing is one currently being argued against; **that does not make its absence a virtue, and it is not offered as one** — the cost below is unchanged, and the reading is one participant's position in a live thread. It is recorded because #70 showed the favourable side of this dossier is the side that goes stale, and leaving out a development that cuts our way is the same failure as leaving in one that has expired.  Previously it appeared only as a comparison point (#16, `comparison.md` §1.5); it is now answered directly in the clause-level Q&A, because the underlying axis — *does the set you grant over match the set you react over* — generalises to any instrument covering more than copyright **合否**: **「持てない」と「持たない方が良い」を混ぜていないか。** §10.1 が条件を許さないので報復条項は置けない（#174 が訂正済み）。**⚠ 逆側は 2 つ**: 防御的終了は OSD 9 の *restriction* だと論じる参加者が居る（有利）／**終了する抑止は、ライセンスを必要としない特許主張者には効かない**（不利・**どちらも未測定**）。 |
| 47 | **A condition must be satisfiable in every distribution mode the licence defines, and this is where condition-bearing AI licences break.** In the same review, a licence defined "Distribution" to include hosted and API access expressly, but its attribution condition required notices "with" the distribution. The submitter conceded hosted access "falls within 'Distribution' as defined" and concluded that **no obligation arises** because "there is no Licensed Material to attach the License to" — a defensible answer that is also an admission that the condition is inert in the delivery mode that dominates AI deployment | **[CLOSED]** **ACD-1.0 avoids this structurally, and the avoidance is now explained rather than assumed.** §10.1 and §4.3 impose no condition in respect of the Work, so nothing's satisfiability depends on how the Work reaches someone; §10.5 and §16.6 confine §16's conditions to the licence text as a document. **This is a case where the absence of conditions is not merely simpler but avoids a failure mode**, and it is recorded here rather than in the favourable material because the same structure produces #41 (a weaker disclaimer) and #16 (no defensive termination) **構造的に当たらず、その理由が記録されている** —— §10.1 / §4.3 が条件を課さないので、充足可能性が配信形態に依存しない。**これ以上潰すものが無い。** |

| 48 | **Our own review method produced a false negative, and the method — not the instance — is the finding.** #45 recorded that a connecting sentence was "missing from the text" when §10.5 and §16.6 say exactly what it claimed was absent. The cause was reading clauses individually rather than following what each one points at: §11.1 arrives before §10.5, and a reader who stops at the apparent conflict never reaches the resolution. **The same failure mode produces the opposite error just as easily** — asserting that the text says something it does not | **[CLOSED]** **Method changed, not just the entry.** All absence claims across the dossier were swept (both languages) and no second instance was found. More usefully, the underlying structure is now documented: six clauses disclaim being conditions, and they cross-reference into a single mesh centred on §10.1. That mesh is what a clause-by-clause reading misses. **A reviewer who reads in the same order will hit the same apparent conflict**, which is why it is now explained in `clause-reference.md` rather than left to be rediscovered **潰し方は実行済み** —— 不在の主張を両言語で全掃引し第 2 例は出ず、mesh の構造そのものが文書化された。**残っているのは「読む順に依存する」という #45 側の論点で、それは草案で閉じている。** |

| 49 | **For the use case this instrument is written for, the licence answers only half the question.** ACD-1.0 exists for works "meant to be learned from", and such works are frequently data about people. §11.4 states plainly that permissions required by data-protection, privacy, publicity and personality law "are not the Dedicator's to give". So an adopter who clears every right this instrument reaches may still be unable to train lawfully — **and nothing in the licence tells them that until they reach §11.4** | **[TRADE]** **Correct, unavoidable, and now stated in the practical guidance rather than only in the clause.** No licence can confer a data-protection basis: under GDPR the controller identifies its own lawful basis, which is not something an upstream publisher can grant in advance. An instrument that blurred this would give users a reassurance that does not exist. `faq.md` A28 now separates the two layers and says which one this licence settles. **What the adopter can usefully do is describe the material — what data it contains, where it came from, on what basis it was collected — and that is not something §10.1 requires of anyone** **どのライセンスにもできないことを、できると偽らない選択である。** GDPR の下では管理者が自らの法的根拠を特定するので、上流が事前に付与できるものではない。**合否は「付与していないと述べ切れているか」**で、§11.4 と `faq.md` A28 がそれを述べている。**費用は消えない。** |

| 50 | **The operative text never calls itself a software licence, which is measurable and unhelpful to us.** "program" and "executable" appear **zero** times; "software" appears twice, both in explanatory passages, **never in a grant or a condition**; "source code" appears once, as one of six items in §1.2's description of what a Work may be. If the criterion for review is that OSI evaluates software licences, an instrument whose operative provisions never mention software sits further outside it than one that does — and the word counts are on the reviewer's side, not ours | **[TRADE]** **Accepted; it is the price of the design and it is now measured rather than asserted.** A licence that says "software" in its grants must be stretched to cover a dataset or a set of weights, and stretching is where ambiguity enters. §1.7 defines Computational Use over the Work rather than over programs, so §6.1 and §8.4 apply identically to code and to a corpus. **What the counts settle is that this is not a software licence with data provisions attached, and does not read as one** — which is exactly what #37 says is the risk **合否**: **「ソフトウェアのライセンスにデータ条項を足したものではない」と測定で述べ切れているか。** 語の出現数（program 0 / executable 0 / software 2）がそれを支える。**⚠ 逆側**: OSI がソフトウェアのライセンスを審査する場だとすれば、**この測定は我々を線から遠ざける** ——**その読みも同じ節に書いてある。** |

| 51 | **Every statement in the dossier about the outside world was undated.** Twenty-one claims describe things that can change — SPDX registration, adopter count, what other instruments are doing, whether any AI-specific licence has been approved — and none carried the date it was checked. Against a review measured in months to years, an undated "not registered" cannot be judged true or false by a reader six months later. **An unjudgeable claim is worse than a wrong one**, because it invites the reader to discount the claims around it | **[CLOSED]** **Fixed structurally rather than line by line.** `AS-OF.md` collects them with a verification date and a re-check method each; the text's own properties are deliberately excluded, since those are settled by the digest check rather than by a date. What remains unresolved is the discipline: **updating a date without re-verifying the value would be the worst possible edit**, and nothing enforces that beyond saying so |

| 52 | **The readiness document claimed a state that the record then contradicted.** `READY-TO-SUBMIT.md` said the text had been read "until findings reached zero", ending with "final read-through: 0 findings — this is where the judgement was made". Adverse facts have gone on accumulating since, and every errata entry was found after it (the counts are deliberately not repeated here — this list and `errata.md` are the authority, and a number written into prose is stale the day after, which is #55's lesson applied to itself), including one place where the text tells a reader to do something incorrect (§16.1). **A reviewer reading that document beside this one finds the first refuted by the second**, which puts the readiness judgement itself in question | **[CLOSED]** **Corrected, and the correction is the more useful record.** The 2026-08-24 judgement was not wrong; the word "zero" was. It meant "the lenses available that day produced nothing new", not "no defects remain" — and lenses arrive later (the ones added since produced everything above). The right standard for submitting was never "zero defects", which would mean never submitting; it is **"nothing known is being concealed"**, and that held then and holds now. **Writing "zero" without stating the standard reads as swapping the standard**, which is the defect worth recording |

| 53 | **The entry page told reviewers that documents they can read are in a language they cannot.** `REVIEWERS.md` headed its list "Map of the Japanese documents" and put eight files under it. Measured by character counts, **four of those are English or answer-in-English**: `comparison.md` (19,289 Latin characters against 1,860 Japanese), `review-responses-clauses.md` (22,738 against 2,836), and both `review-responses.md` and `review-responses-meta.md`, whose every answer is given in English with Japanese only in the notes explaining the phrasing. **A reviewer following that page would have skipped the clause-level objections and the licence comparison believing they could not read them** | **[CLOSED]** **Corrected by measurement, and the measurement is now stated on the page.** The map gives a language per file and says which documents are already in English. **This was invisible to every check run so far** — they were all searches for patterns *within* a language, and none asked what language a file was in. The finding also reverses the assumption behind the page: the material a reviewer is most likely to want was already in English, and the page was directing them away from it |

| 54 | **The index built to make 140 entries findable was itself the second-least findable document here.** Measured by inbound links between the files in this directory: `QUESTION-INDEX.md` had **2** (only the two entry pages), `AS-OF.md` had **4**, while the bodies they index had 6–11 each. **Nothing in the twelve substantive documents pointed at either.** A reviewer who opens a file directly — the normal way to read a repository on the web — would never learn the index exists, which is precisely the readership the index was written for | **[CLOSED]** **Fixed by giving every body document a footer pointing at the index, the staleness table, and the adverse case.** The finding matters more than the fix: **this was invisible to every check so far because all of them read files, and none measured the shape of the links between them.** Content-level checks cannot see a topology problem. Adding an entry point does not help if only the entry points know about it |

| 55 | **Twelve files declared a last-updated date older than their actual last edit**, by up to ten days — and they did so immediately after `AS-OF.md` established that a date should record when something was *verified*, not when it was written. An existing check (65) validated that these dates are well-formed ISO-8601 but never compared them to reality. **A stale date makes a document look untouched when it has been revised**, and in a review measured in months that is one of the few signals a reader has about currency | **[CLOSED]** **Corrected, and the enforcement failed twice before working.** The first version omitted an import, so every file threw and was swallowed by a `continue` — it reported "20 files consistent" while examining none, and passed only because it examined nothing. The second version examined correctly and **failed CI**: requiring the declared date to be at least the git date is unsatisfiable, because **the commit that fixes a date advances that file's git date**, and a sweeping change (adding a footer to twelve files) advances the git date of documents whose content nobody touched. The check now runs **ADVISORY with a 14-day tolerance**, on the reasoning that the frontmatter date means *when the content was last reviewed*, not *when a byte last changed*. **Two failure modes in one check, and the second only surfaced in CI** — local runs were green because the local tree had not been touched by the sweep |

**What this exercise changed.** Two dossier statements were wrong and have been corrected
(#15, #24 — the second of them a misdescription of which section grants the licence, which had
been repeated across the submission packet). Two criticisms of the text are accepted as fair and
left unrepaired because the text is frozen (#18, #20), with #22 a third if the looser reading is
taken. Three are costs that were understated and are now stated (#16, #17, #21). **Nothing
raised was dismissed**, and three rounds together moved the count of adverse facts from 14 to
**51** — which is the honest direction for a list like this to move as it is examined. The third round was not prompted by anyone: it audited the dossier's own clause citations against the text, on the theory that the §3/§4 error (#24) was found only because someone finally read the clause, and that others of the same kind were likely.

## What two dimensions borrowed from an outside review produced (2026-09-05)

Two observations arrived from the OSI reviews of other instruments: the ModelGo submitter's
interpretation that hosted-only delivery — where the remote user receives Output and never receives
the Licensed or Derivative Materials — triggers no licence-copy or notice obligation, together with
the drafting history that a notice requirement on Output had been **deleted** in March 2025 once it
was seen that it could become a condition on mere use; and a decomposition, put to the OpenMDW
thread, of where a litigation-termination clause first crosses an open-source principle.

**Neither produced a new adverse fact.** Both had already been reduced on 2026-09-04, as #46 and
#47. What they carried that was new were two *methods*: trace a concrete delivery mode until an
obligation fires or does not, and follow a defined term across the sections that use it rather than
the one that defines it. Applied to this text, those two produced the entries below.

The material was worth more as a way of measuring than as a set of facts. That is the thesis
`BLIND-SPOTS.md` was written on, and this is the first time it has been tested with material from
outside. Neither dimension appears in that file's candidate list — which is the point it makes
about itself: a dimension one can enumerate in advance is one already used.

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 56 | **The artifact SPDX registration requires may be the thing §16.4 forbids.** §16.4 permits copying the text verbatim and forbids distributing it "in modified form under the name ... or under the identifier 'ACD-1.0'", so that name and identifier keep denoting one fixed text; §16.5 carves out translations and nothing else. `ACD-1.0.spdx.xml` carries `licenseId="ACD-1.0"` and the full name, and is not verbatim — every clause is reflowed into a `<p>` with the line breaks and indentation of the fixed text removed, and the header element carries an `<alt match=".+">` substitution point. **So the registration path, on a strict reading, requires producing what the licence prohibits** | **[CLOSED]** **Unresolved, and unfixable while frozen** (`errata.md` E8). A sensible reading says a re-encoding is not a modification, and nobody has raised it. But the text does not say so, and the licence's own §6.5 — a permission an automated system cannot determine is not a permission — is the strongest argument that the machine-readable form should have been contemplated rather than left to inference. **Found by asking what forms the text is redistributed in, not by reading §16** **✅ CLOSED 2026-09-23（草案の条文で実測）**: `ACD-1.2-DRAFT.txt` §16.4 が ***"A re-encoding of the text into a machine-readable …"*** を持ち、**機械可読形への再符号化が modified text に当たらないことを条文が述べている**（errata E8）。**§6.5 の基準を自分の §16 へ当てた結果が本文に入った。** 1.0 は凍結のまま。 |
| 57 | **One sentence in the no-condition mesh is unqualified where its six neighbours are qualified.** §10.1 says "in respect of the Work"; §10.5 says nothing in Section 16 "is a condition upon Your use of the Work", and §11.3 and §11.4 each say a clause "is not a condition upon You"; §16.6 says §16.4-16.5 "bind no recipient of the Work". §10.4's second sentence says flatly that the instrument "contains nothing that You could breach" — and "You" is defined at §1.4 as **any person or entity exercising permissions under this Dedication**, §16.4 grants a permission over the text, and §16.5 states in terms that its proviso **is** "a condition upon distributing the translated text". **A translator-distributor is "You", and does have something to breach** | **[CLOSED]** **Real, narrow, and unfixable while frozen** (`errata.md` E9). Nothing about what anyone may do with the Work changes, and #27 and #45 already answer the substantive version of this through §10.1's qualifier. What is new is that the qualifier is absent from exactly one sentence, and that a reviewer following the **defined term** rather than the section arrives there. **Found by following "You" through §1.4, §16.4 and §16.5 — the sections that use the term, not the section that is about conditions** **✅ CLOSED 2026-09-23（草案の条文で実測）**: §10.4 第 2 文が ***"This Dedication, **in respect of the Work**, contains no termination provision …"*** となり、**6 つの隣人と同じ限定を得た**（errata E9）。1.0 は凍結のまま。 |
| 58 | **The entry page understated the disclosure by a factor of four, in the direction that looks like concealment.** `REVIEWERS.md` is the English page a reviewer reads first, and it declared "**118** worked entries", "**All 14** adverse facts" and "**Five** imprecisions" while the files behind those links held 144, 57 and 9 at the moment of the sweep (this row's own addition moved two of them again, which is the point). The word that does the damage is "All": it is a completeness claim, and a reviewer who opens a list four times longer than the page promised has been given a reason to distrust the page rather than the list. **Every one of the three erred low** — a dossier whose argument is "we disclose everything" cannot afford to under-report what it discloses | **[CLOSED]** **Fixed, and the class was already known.** Check 460 exists precisely because "a document states its own scale and the statement is stale the day after"; it covered the submission packet, the FAQ, the clause reference, `against.md` and the index — **and not the page written last and read first**. It now covers `REVIEWERS.md` and `READY-TO-SUBMIT.md` too. **Found by sweeping every declared count in the directory after correcting one of them**, which is the discipline of #55 applied to a second kind of moving number |

## What the machine-readable layer's own claims produced (2026-09-05)

A third dimension from the same round, and the only one that was on `BLIND-SPOTS.md`'s candidate
list: **check every claim the machine-readable descriptor makes against the clause it cites.**
The descriptor exists because §6.5 holds that a permission an automated system cannot determine is
no permission — so a licence saying that ought to be determinable by machine, and this repository's
whole thesis is machine-readable authority. If any layer here should be exact, it is that one.

**Most of it was clean, and the clean part is worth stating.** All 33 clause citations resolve
(Check 451a already guarantees this, and it does), and every one of the 29 value-claims matches
what the cited clause says — permissions, the six `requirements: false`, warranty and liability.
Nothing was overstated.

**What the dimension found was not an inaccuracy but a selection.**

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 59 | **The descriptor enumerates six things the instrument does not do, and omits the one thing it does.** `reservationsAndLimits` covers TDM reservation, patent retaliation, field-of-use restriction, termination, trademark grant and governing law — **all six are absences**. The one restriction in the document, on redistributing the licence *text* in modified form under its name or identifier (§16.4, with §16.5's translation proviso), has no key at all — while §16's *permission* side does (`reusableByAnyone` → §16.3). **A consumer built on the descriptor concludes the document restricts nothing**, which is true of the Work and false of the text. The file's closing line, "Any adopter of ACD-1.0 may copy this file verbatim", is §16.4 applied to itself, phrased as a permission rather than as a limit | **[CLOSED]** **Recorded, not fixed** (`errata.md` E10) — `machine.json` is pinned by Check 453. Honestly bounded: no user of the Work is misled about their own obligations (§16.4 binds no recipient — §16.6), the descriptor says in terms that it is non-operative and the text governs, and licence scanners rarely model restrictions on a licence text at all. It is here because **§6.5's reasoning cuts both ways** — a restriction an automated system cannot determine is one it will breach without knowing — and because the omission runs in the favourable direction, which is the direction that needs stating **✅ CLOSED 2026-09-24（descriptor の現物で実測）** —— `ACD-1.1.machine.json` の `reservationsAndLimits` は **`textRedistribution`（`clause: "16.4"`）を持つ**。**7 つ目の key が、この文書が現に持つ唯一の制限を述べている。** 1.0 の descriptor は凍結のまま（Check 453 が byte を pin しており、直すことは凍結の趣旨に反する）。 **⚠ E10 は errata では open のまま** ——**「版ごとに閉じ直す」種類**だからである。**再発の瞬間は「新しい版の descriptor を作るとき」なので、そこを Check 468h で押さえた**（1.0 以外のすべての `ACD-*.machine.json` に §16.4 の key を要求・**非 vacuity 実測済み**）。 |

## What reading order produced (2026-09-05)

The fourth dimension of the round, and the one #45 pointed at: **what does a reviewer actually
meet first?** #45 was a reading-order defect inside the licence (§11.1 arrives before the §10.5 that
reconciles it); the same question can be asked of the directory.

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 60 | **The page GitHub renders when a reviewer opens `LICENSES/` contained no English sentence at all.** `LICENSES/README.md` is the directory's landing page, and measured by character class it is 885 Japanese characters against 1,248 Latin ones — **all of the Latin being filenames and identifiers, with not one English sentence in the file**. The English entry point is named on line 30, inside a Japanese table cell reading 「審査者が最初に読む英語の入口」; a reviewer who cannot read Japanese **cannot tell that cell from the others** and would have to guess from the filename. #53 corrected exactly this class one level down — the map inside `REVIEWERS.md` claiming documents were Japanese when four were English — and **nobody checked the page a reviewer lands on before reaching that map** | **[CLOSED]** **Fixed, and enforced.** An English block now sits directly under the heading, pointing at `REVIEWERS.md`, the authoritative text, and this list; the whole file is deliberately not translated, which remains the stated policy. **Check 459b** requires an English pointer in the first 20 lines, because Check 459 only ever asked whether documents were *listed* — **listed and reachable are different properties when the reader cannot read the list**. Measured honestly: the root `README.md` does carry an English reviewer callout directly under its badges, so the failure was specific to the directory page |

## What three more dimensions produced (2026-09-05)

Three measurements, one of which found something. The other two are recorded because **a clean
result is only useful if it is written down** — otherwise the next pass re-measures it.

**Length distribution (clean, and it corroborates the dossier's own account).** Measured by
characters per section: §1 Definitions 13.4%, §8 Patents 12.9%, §2 Scope 12.4% are the largest;
§8 is by far the densest per clause (577 characters against a median near 300). §3 — the
dedication itself, the operative act the instrument is named for — is **670 characters, 2.5%,
three clauses**, the second-shortest section in the document. That inversion looks alarming until
it is read with the negation counts below: §3 is short *and* the least defensive section in the
text (0.9 negations per 100 words, the lowest). It is short because a surrender is a simple act.
What a reviewer opening at §3 does not get is the argument that makes it survivable — that lives
in §4.4 and §2 — which is **the same reading-order problem already recorded as #45 / errata E7**,
not a new one.

**Negation density (clean, and informative).** §10 Absence of Conditions is highest at 7.5 per
100 words, which is what a section of absences should look like. Second is **§9
Machine-Generated Material at 5.3** — short (3.6% of the text) but densely defensive, which is
the signature of drafting around something that cannot be asserted positively. That is exactly
what §9 is: the submission says it is built "the other way round", not presuming a right exists.
**The measurement agrees with the account we already give**, which is the outcome worth recording.

**File-size distribution (clean).** `against.md` is the largest file in the directory (61 KB) and
is also the one reviewers are told to read first. That combination would be a defect if the worst
facts were buried, but the two entries marked unanswerable are **#1 and #2** — the first two rows.
The round-based sections that follow are chronological by the append-only rule. No change.

**Audience coverage — this one found something, and not about audiences.**

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 61 | **The staleness layer's coverage was defined by "files that have the field", so the three files whose currency matters most were silently exempt.** Check 461b compares each `LICENSES/*.md` frontmatter `last-updated` against git, and skips any file without that line (`if not _m461: continue`). Measured 2026-09-05: **3 of 20 files had no frontmatter at all** — and they were `ACD-1.0.submission.md` (the submitted packet), `FROZEN.md` (the single source for the freeze digests and the venue), and `READY-TO-SUBMIT.md` (the judgement that the text was ready). **The check examined 17 files and printed a clean result**, the shape a skip-on-missing branch always takes: it makes a coverage hole silent while printing a clean result | **[CLOSED]** **Fixed and enforced.** The three files now declare `file` / `audience` / `last-updated` / `canonical-ref` like every other document here. **Check 461c** (BLOCKING) requires the field to exist — coverage is a static property that does not move when a file is edited, so it can be blocking even though 461b itself must stay advisory (#55: the date moves as a side effect of editing, the field's existence does not). Found by measuring **declared audiences**, which is not what it found: three files declared none, and the reason turned out to be that they had no frontmatter for any layer to read |

## An evaluation frame we have not analysed (2026-09-05)

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 62 | *(Corrected 2026-09-06: **read at source on that date**; the reason given for not reading it — that this environment could not fetch — was itself false, see #76.)* **The frame reviewers of the adjacent submissions are using, we have never applied to this text.** Our own observation of the OpenMDW and ModelGo threads records that **OSAID (the Open Source AI Definition) is referenced as an evaluation frame**, and that systematic OSAID-based comparison is barely published. This dossier mentions OSAID exactly twice: once in that observation, and once in `submission-reference.md` §1c to say the submission is *not* asking the list to decide anything about it. **There is no analysis.** A reviewer arriving from those threads may reasonably ask how an instrument written for works meant to be learned from stands in relation to it, and the honest answer today is that we have not looked | **[CLOSED]** **Now filled, and the definitional consequence is confirmed from source rather than inferred.** OSAID v1.0 conditions all three required components on OSI approval in terms — *"Data Information shall be made available under OSI-approved terms"*, *"Code … under OSI-approved licenses"*, *"Parameters … under OSI-approved terms"*. **So an AI system cannot be OSAID-conformant while any of those three is released under ACD-1.0, until ACD-1.0 is approved** — and those three are exactly what §6, §7 and §9 were written for. That is adverse: it means the instrument is unusable for its own target use case in the one framework the field is converging on. It is also why approval is load-bearing here rather than ornamental, which is stated in `review-responses-meta.md` and not argued as a reason to approve. One thing is deliberately left open: OSAID says "terms" for two components and "licenses" for one, and **we do not resolve what that asymmetry means.** Originally:  Two things can be said without reading anything. First, a **definitional consequence**: where any requirement is phrased in terms of an OSI-approved licence, an unapproved instrument cannot satisfy it — so for that class of requirement ACD-1.0's answer is fixed by #12 and #13 and does not depend on the text of the frame. Second, a **scoping point**: OSAID evaluates AI *systems*, and a licence is not a system; the relevant question is whether ACD-1.0 is a suitable instrument for the components such a system releases — weights, parameters, data — which is squarely what §6, §7 and §9 were written for. **What we deliberately do not do is state what OSAID requires.** We have not read its current text in this environment, and restating a live standard from memory is the specific failure this dossier's `AS-OF.md` exists to prevent (see the entry added there, with the re-check method). **Recorded as an adverse fact rather than a to-do**, because a reviewer's question is not answered by our intention to answer it later **潰し方は実行済み** —— OSAID を原典で読み、3 要素すべてが OSI 承認条件付きであることを逐語で確認して反映した。**「OSAID 適合は承認の条件ではない」は `board-decisions.md` が理事会の記録から別途確定させている。** |

## What an outside reader found that our own mechanisation had not (2026-09-06)

An observation arrived from outside with **no location, no number, and no document named** — only
that at least one self-declared count still disagreed with reality, and that it had survived the
recent broad mechanisation. It was right, and there was more than one.

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 63 | **Four self-declared counts were stale, all of them just outside the fence the check was built to defend.** `docs/files/LICENSES/` mirrors said the adverse list held "14 件" (62) and the errata "不正確さ 5 件（E1〜E5）" (E1–E10) and "54 件の不利な事実" (62); `CLAUDE.md` §7 said the blind-spot file recorded "10 次元と未使用の候補 10 件" against an actual **17 and 3** — and the candidate figure erred in the direction that promises a future reader more unused ways to measure than exist. **The reason they survived is the more useful finding than the four instances.** Check 460 grew one face at a time, and *each face was added after a specific place had been caught drifting* — the submission packet, the FAQ, the clause reference, the FAQ mirror, `against.md`, the index, and (the same day) the entry pages. **Its coverage was therefore "places we have seen drift", never "places that can drift"** | **[CLOSED]** **Fixed, and the design changed rather than the four lines.** Restating the dossier's scale is now **forbidden** in `docs/files/LICENSES/` (Check 460 face (h)) instead of compared, because a mirror describes *what a file is* and a size claim there has no reader value while its drift is certain — a comparison face would have created one more thing to maintain and one more way to be wrong. Inside `LICENSES/` the counts do have reader value (a reviewer wants scale), so those stay under the comparing faces. `CLAUDE.md` §7 now states no number and points at the file. **No check was added for `CLAUDE.md`**: it legitimately quotes past measurements as history, and separating history from a live claim is a judgement a regular expression cannot make — the same reason recorded in `check-repository-consistency-map.md` §2.9 for prose path references |

## The same sweep, one document further in (2026-09-06)

#63 fixed four stale counts outside the fence. Continuing the sweep **inside** it found two more,
in the worst possible place.

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 64 | **The message written to be sent to `license-review` misstated two of the numbers in the section whose entire purpose is that a machine can check them.** `submission-reference.md` §4c is our answer to "no lawyer has read it": a table of properties that *can* be established without counsel, each with the command that establishes it. **Three of its rows were wrong** — "**50 entries, 1–50, no gaps**" for an adverse list that holds **63**; "**29 / 29**" clause pointers in the machine-readable descriptor where the repository's own Check 451a counts **33** on every run; and "lowest is `Contribution` at **4**" for defined-term usage where the lowest is **3**, and is shared with `Machine-Generated Material` — so that row was wrong about the number *and* about which term it identified. **A reviewer who runs the commands we published gets different numbers from the ones we published**, and that turns the section into evidence for the opposite of what it argues. Only the first moves as this list grows; **the other two could not have drifted at all** — their inputs are the frozen text and the pinned descriptor — so they were wrong when they were written, which is a different failure from staleness and one that no amount of re-checking a *moving* number would have caught | **[CLOSED]** **Corrected, and enforced (Check 460 face (i)).** Face (a) already read this file — but only one sentence of it, the one that counts worked entries — so §4c sat inside the fence and outside the check.
