## What survives

After the list above, the claim this submission actually makes is narrow, and it is the only one
it makes:

> The text is internally coherent, contains no project-specific or replaceable content, grants
> permissions in licence terms in every jurisdiction (dedication where dedication works, licence
> where it does not), and reserves nothing. Whether that is enough to meet the Open Source
> Definition is the question being asked — not a conclusion being asserted.

Everything mechanically checkable about the text has been checked and is recorded with the
commands to re-check it (submission §4c). Everything legal is unchecked, and no amount of care
in the first category substitutes for the second.

### What has held up, stated with the same discipline as the list above
| 223 | **The Check that verifies the reviewer's verification command did not run the reviewer's verification command.** Check 472c executes the commands `REVIEWERS.md` gives a reviewer and compares the output against the expected value printed beside them — the layer added after #190 found that page's expected values disagreeing with reality. **Measured 2026-09-24: before running each command it rewrote `shasum -a 256 -c` into `sha256sum -c`.** On Linux the two produce identical output, so CI stayed green and the substitution was invisible. On macOS `sha256sum -c` **does not fall back to standard input when the operand is omitted** — it prints its usage line and verifies nothing — so the freeze check reported *expected 5× OK / got 0× OK* **while the command actually printed on the entry page verified all five digests correctly**. The page was right and the instrument was wrong, and the error was in the direction that costs the most to diagnose: a red gate about the frozen licence text, on the machine where the work is done. **The converse is the part that matters**: had the entry page carried the broken form, this Check would have rewritten it into the working one and reported green — **a gate that substitutes its own input cannot detect a defect in that input** | **[CLOSED]** **The substitution is removed; the command runs verbatim, and the docstring records why.** If `shasum` is absent from an environment the command emits nothing and the Check goes red with the count mismatch, which is the honest outcome — the entry page would then be giving that environment a command it cannot run. **This is #87's lesson on the instrument, applied to an instrument built three days earlier by us**: the first sweep counted licence submissions with a regex that dropped `\bAI\b`, and the correction there was *report the tool beside the number*. Here the tool changed the object before measuring it. **⚠ What this does not establish**: that the other three commands on the page are executed verbatim for the right reasons — they are, and they were never rewritten, but that is because no rewrite happened to be convenient for them, not because a rule forbade it. The rule now exists in the docstring and nowhere else. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 置換をやめ、入口ページの文字列をそのまま実行する。**合否**: **審査者が貼る文字列と、Check が 実行する文字列が同一か。** 片方でも書き換えれば、その Check は入口ページではなく自分の書き換えを検査している。 |
| 224 | **The self-check that verifies the safety net stopped reaching a verdict, and the failure mode is neither red nor green.** `mutation-probe.yml` is the only layer that verifies the Checks actually catch regressions rather than merely existing; it runs weekly and does not gate any pull request. **Measured 2026-09-24 from a dispatched run: the consistency job reached 368 of 372 mutations and hit `timeout-minutes: 30`, ending as `cancelled` with four left.** Cancelled is not failure — **no `All N caught` line is printed, so the run neither confirms the net nor reports a gap**, and nothing goes red for anyone to rerun. **The behaviour job met this exact wall on 2026-08-17 at 55 minutes and was sharded, first four ways and then eight; its sibling was never sharded at all.** The workflow's own comment block records that history in full, three lines above a job that had been left single. **Fixing the layer that hit the wall and leaving the one beside it means meeting the same wall a second time.** The consistency job also never printed an elapsed line, which is the one signal that would have shown the duration growing toward the limit — **the measurement that exists on the sibling and would have made this visible months earlier** | **[CLOSED]** **Sharded four ways, and the elapsed line added.** Coverage was verified rather than assumed: the four shards together cover all 372 mutations exactly once (`index % n`, checked by enumeration). Measured cost before sharding was **4.5s per mutation locally and 4.9s in CI**, from which a shard was estimated at about 7.6 minutes. **That estimate was wrong and the run that followed says so**: the real shards took **426 / 549 / 680 / 714 seconds — a maximum of 11.9 minutes** against the 30-minute limit (2.5× headroom, not 4×), with a **1.68× spread** between shards. Two written premises fell with it: that consistency mutations cost the same each (they do not, though the spread is smaller than the 2× the behaviour side saw), and that per-mutation cost would be unchanged — **the four shards together spent 6.4s per mutation against the single job's 4.9s**, because each shard pays its own checkout and setup. **Sharding buys wall-clock, not total work.** Shard 3/4 was run end to end: **93 mutations, all caught, working tree restored.** The scope line now names the shard (`All 93 mutations in shard 3/4 (of 372 total)`), because a shard that claims the full count lets a reader conclude the net is healthy from a log that verified a quarter of it — **the same false-scope defect that was fixed on the behaviour side in 2026-08-17 and, again, not carried across.** **⚠ What this does not establish**: that the four mutations never reached were passing. They were not run, and the correct statement is that their status is unknown until the next weekly run. **⚠ The converse**: sharding buys headroom, it does not stop the growth. Every mutation added moves the layer back toward the limit, and the elapsed line is now the thing that must actually be read. **潰し方は実行済み** —— consistency 側も 4 分割し、elapsed を必ず出す。**合否**: **週次 run が `cancelled` でなく success か failure で終わっているか。** `cancelled` は「まだ失敗していない」ではなく**結論が無い**状態であり、週次ゆえ誰も rerun しないので、そのまま安全網の検証が止まる。 |
| 225 | **We published a machine-readable index that claimed conformance to a schema which does not exist, and wrote a BLOCKING Check enforcing rules we attributed to a specification nobody here has ever read.** `.well-known/index.json` and `.well-known/agent-skills/index.json` have declared `"$schema": "https://schemas.agentskills.io/discovery/0.2.0/schema.json"` since 2026-08-23, and `.well-known/api-catalog` pointed at the same URL as that resource's `service-desc`. **Measured 2026-09-25, four ways, each with a control**: the host does not exist — `agentskills.io`'s own authoritative nameservers return **NXDOMAIN with the parent SOA** for it while the parent resolves; the Wayback Machine holds **no snapshot of that URL, ever**; the Agent Skills specification site defines **no discovery index, no `.well-known` path and no `$schema`** across all nine pages of its own documentation index; and the official `agentskills/agentskills` repository contains **zero occurrences of `schemas.agentskills.io` and zero of `well-known`** (controls: `SKILL.md` returns 19, a nonsense token returns 0, so the search discriminates). **Check 448 then encoded this**: its docstring stated that *the specification requires name / type / description / url / digest on every entry* and added *"必須 field 名は本 Check にリテラルで持つ (外部仕様なので repo から導出できない)"* — **asserting an external authority for a rule we invented**, and enforcing the presence of the unreachable `$schema` as *"仕様上 required"*. The entries also declared `"type": "skill-md"` while pointing at `llms-full.txt` (plain text) and `AI2AI.md` (no skill frontmatter) — **neither is a SKILL.md in the sense the standard defines**. **This is #77 on the machine-facing surface**: there, the submission requirements were reconstructed from neighbours' experience instead of read at source; here, an entire discovery format was | **[CLOSED]** **The false claims are removed and the Check now says whose rule it is.** `$schema` is gone from both indexes, the dead `service-desc` context is gone from the catalog (8 → 7), and `type` is `document`. The five fields are **kept** — they are good — but the docstring now records that the basis moved from *the specification requires* to *we decided*, and cites the one thing the specification does say that supports them: progressive disclosure, where an agent judges relevance from name and description before fetching the body. **New 448b** forbids a `$schema` outside the origin we serve, with the origin derived from `sitemap.xml` rather than hard-coded; **non-vacuity was proved with the exact declaration this entry is about**. **And the deferred half of #212 now has an answer.** That entry said *"we cannot verify offline whether the discovery schema defines a licence field"* and declined to invent a key — correctly. The specification does define one, and it is not on any index: **`license` is an optional field of `SKILL.md` frontmatter**. The repository's one real skill carries it now. **⚠ What this does not establish**: that the host never existed. NXDOMAIN today from its own authoritative zone, plus no archival trace and no mention in the standard's site or repository, is what was measured; *never* is a stronger word than that evidence. **⚠ The converse, at the same weight.** Publishing our own discovery file under `.well-known/` is not itself wrong — the namespace is extensible, and everything an agent actually uses (name, description, URL, digest) was correct and resolvable the whole time. **Nothing here was unlicensed or unreachable**; what was false was the claim of conformance. And Check 448's *effects* were good: it is why `description` exists on every entry and why the digest prefix is the standard `sha256:`. **The defect was the provenance of the authority, not the content of the rules** — which is the harder kind to see, because everything it produced looked right. **⚠ Removing a false signal is not the same as installing a true one, and the first version of this fix did only the former.** The C6 review named three losses of one shape: an agent could no longer attempt schema validation *at all* (before, it would at least fail loudly); `document` carries less signal than `skill-md` would have carried *had it been real*; and the catalog no longer said this resource has a description. **All three were backfilled rather than accepted**: both indexes now carry a `documentation` field and the catalog's `service-desc` is restored, all three pointing at the mirror document that describes the format — **measured at 200 on the published site**, not assumed. **The trap was obvious once named**: declaring a pointer that nothing verifies is the defect this entry is about, so **448c requires the target to resolve to a file in the repository**, proved RED both by pointing it at a missing file and by dropping the field. **What is still not installed is machine-checkable structure** — the description is prose for a reader, not a schema a validator can run. That is the honest remaining gap. **⚠ The sharpest part is that the discipline meant to prevent this produced it.** On 2026-08-23 the same fetch failed, and the increment note for that day records stopping to ask whether the failure was evidence about the tool or about the world — the right question, recorded as a near-miss avoided. **The conclusion written that day was that Agent Skills is a real standard and *"the URL was as the specification says"*.** The first half was checked. **The second half was never checked at all** — it was inferred from the first. **Suspecting the instrument correctly leaves you with *undetermined*, not with *fine*,** and the note that day converted one into the other. That note now carries a dated correction; the original text is kept. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 存在しない schema への適合主張を消し、Check の典拠を「仕様の要求」から「我々の設計判断」へ書き直し、規範層の check-map が述べていた「**仕様原文で確認**」も訂正した。**合否**: **外部の仕様を典拠として引くとき、その仕様を実際に開いたか。** 開けないなら、その規則は我々のものとして書く。 |
| 226 | **We audited the classification of the entries we had marked as not ours to close, and three of them said, in their own text, that they were not external — and the error ran in the comfortable direction.** Every category this register can close itself had reached zero (`TEXT` / `DOSSIER` / `OPEN` / `UNTRIAGED`), leaving 51 `EXTERNAL` and 28 `TRADE`. **`EXTERNAL` is the most comfortable label available** — it says an item is neither our fault nor our work — so the classification was re-read adversarially against that bias, which is the discipline #140 records and the owner named on 2026-09-13. **Three entries contradicted their own token.** #181's disposition reads *"`EXTERNAL` に分類したのは初回の自動導出の誤りで"* and calls its remedy done. #187's reads *"開示の正確さは我々の側の作業である（外部依存ではない）。⚠ この entry は `EXTERNAL` ではなく実質 `DOSSIER`"*. #192's reads *"注記の有無を機械で見る層が無いことを認めること —— 現在これは習慣であって強制ではない"* — **naming a gate we could build and had not**. **The audit then found the failure #192 describes happening a second time.** `AS-OF.md`'s falsification table states its own rule — *"観測が来たら、まずここを見る"* — and #192 records a row whose condition was satisfied for nine days with nobody going back. **Measured 2026-09-25: #211 had sat in this register since the previous day carrying a senior list participant's statement that existing licences already cover the third gap** (Fontana, *"appropriate even in situations where copyrightability is relatively dubious"*), which runs directly at the row asserting that gap is real. **The row had never heard of it.** **And four of the six rows carried no declared state at all** — not met, not evaluable, nothing. Two of those four turn out to be **unevaluable in principle until we submit**, which is itself a fact the table never said. | **[CLOSED]** **The tokens are corrected, the table now declares a dated state per row, and Check 475 makes the second part structural.** #181 → `CLOSED`, #187 → `DOSSIER` (still open, because the family recurred four times — **but *why it is open* and *who can close it* are different questions, and the token answers the second**), #192 → `CLOSED` by building the layer it named. **Every row of the falsification table now carries 🔴 met / 🟡 not met, with a near miss / ⚪ not met / ⚫ precondition unmet, plus an ISO date**, and the #211 near miss is written in rather than written over, per the table's own rule. **⚠ The scope of Check 475 is stated in its own docstring rather than implied**: it prevents a row going quiet and stale; **it cannot make anyone notice**. Whether a condition has been met is a judgement about the world. **No freshness threshold is set** — a check that reddens on a calendar rots on its own and teaches readers to ignore red. **⚠ The converse, which the audit also produced.** The other 48 `EXTERNAL` entries were read and they are honest: each names what only counsel, OSI, a court, or an adopter can do, and what we can do meanwhile. **The classification was not systematically self-serving — three errors in fifty-one.** **⚠ And two of the three were already confessed in their own prose**, which means the register was more honest than its own machine-readable summary — **the prose knew; the token did not.** That is the reverse of the usual failure here, where the summary is confident and the detail is thin. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 分類を是正し、反証表に状態を宣言させ、Check 475 で構造化した。**合否**: **「我々には閉じられない」と分類したものを、その entry 自身の本文が否定していないか。** `EXTERNAL` は最も居心地のよい分類なので、**その山だけは定期的に、逆向きの圧力をかけて読み直す。** |
| 227 | **The check that stops the submission sending a reviewer to a clause that says nothing was reading four of the thirty-five documents the submission sends them to.** Check 472 exists because clause numbers are not preserved across versions — 1.0's §15.7 and §15.8 are absent from every successor — and #144 records the packet having done exactly that once. **Measured 2026-09-25**: the submission packet and entry page link a reviewer to **35 files**; the check's face list was **four**, written as a literal. **The wider surface is currently clean** — nine reviewer-facing answer documents carry 272 clause citations and **every one resolves in the submission target** — so nothing is wrong today. **What is unguarded is the moment of maximum risk**: when `SUBMISSION-TARGET` moves to a successor, documents like `ACD-1.0.faq.md` become documents about a *different* version, and a literal four-face list sees none of it. **This is the same shape as #194 and as Check 473b's rebuild** — a gate whose stated purpose names a risk, scanning a subset of the surfaces where the risk lives. | **[CLOSED]** **The face set is derived (4 → 25) and the widening was proved on the scenario it exists for.** Two rules only, because complicated scoping is where false positives live: files named `<target>.*` are about that version, plus the version-neutral entry and index pages. **Documents carrying another version's name fall out by construction** — `ACD-1.1-CHANGELIST.md` citing 1.1 clauses is correct, and checking it against the target would be a false positive. **Non-vacuity was measured at the version switch, not with a synthetic number.** A made-up clause number (one belonging to no version) does *not* fire, by design: the check only treats a number as a clause if it is a clause in some version, precisely so dossier section numbers are not misread. So the probe was the real scenario — `SUBMISSION-TARGET` set to 1.2 — and it went RED on `READY-TO-SUBMIT.md` and `REVIEWERS.md`. **Stated exactly: the old four-face list would have caught `REVIEWERS.md` on its own; `READY-TO-SUBMIT.md` is the face the widening added.** One face, demonstrated — not twenty-one claimed. **⚠ And widening 472 immediately broke 472b, which was the useful part.** Reusing the derived set for the verbatim-quotation check lit four files and sixteen occurrences, and **reading them showed every one was correct**: `errata.md` quoting the successor's wording is what an errata document *is for*, and `against.md`, `discussion-log.md` and `jurisdictions.md` are records that span versions. **A clause *number* must resolve in the target; a *quotation* may legitimately come from another version in a document whose job is to compare them.** 472b keeps the narrow set, and the measurement is written into the code beside it. **⚠ The converse on the audit that produced this.** The same sweep measured Check 472's coverage at **10.1% of all clause citations in `LICENSES/`** and that number is misleading — the other 89.9% are in documents about other versions, where the narrow scope is correct. **The first framing of a coverage number is usually the wrong one**, and the honest question was not *what fraction does it scan* but *is the set of submission-side faces complete*. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 面の集合を導出型にし、版の切り替えで RED を実測した。**合否**: **走査範囲を literal で書いた Check は、その literal を書いた日の面しか見ない。** 射程が「ある集合のすべて」を名乗るなら、集合は導出する。 |
| 228 | **The base rate that carries this dossier's reading of its own silence had only ever been measured over thirty of the archive's five hundred and six months — and the first attempt to widen it produced a number that was wrong in our favour.** #109 established, over `license-discuss` 2024-01 to 2026-09, that **22% of threads draw no reply at all**, and that figure is load-bearing: it is what licenses our position that the silence following our own post is ordinary rather than diagnostic. **It had never been checked against the rest of the record.** On 2026-09-25 the complete archives of both lists were fetched — **506 months, 199 `license-review` and 307 `license-discuss`, no month missing** — and the rate recomputed. **The first computation said 38.9% on #109's own window, against #109's 22%, and it was wrong.** The detector read only the first physical line of each `Subject:` header; **225 of 491 subject lines in that window are folded across lines**, and the samples show one thread wrapping at *different* points in different messages, so a single conversation was counted as several — most of the fragments single-message, inflating the zero-reply rate. **The error ran toward the conclusion we prefer.** A higher base rate makes our silence more ordinary. | **[CLOSED]** **Fixed, recomputed, and the load-bearing claim survives on an independent instrument.** Unfolding headers and decoding encoded words gives, across the whole record: **`license-review` 618 threads, 150 with no reply — 24.3%**; **`license-discuss` 2,913 threads, 798 with no reply — 27.4%**. **#109's reading holds at census scale**: silence is an ordinary outcome on both lists, at roughly one thread in four, over twenty-seven years rather than thirty months. **⚠ The instruments are not fully reconciled and that is stated rather than smoothed.** On #109's exact window this detector reports 76 threads and 27.6% where #109 reports 68 and 22% — **the same direction and the same order of magnitude, not the same number**. Eight threads separate them, and the residual is not explained; #109 notes excluding a duplicate auto-forward, which accounts for some of it. **Neither figure supersedes the other, and both are quoted with the instrument that produced them** (#87). **⚠ What the census does not do.** It measures *threads*, and #139 established that a thread's silence is not the absence of a decision — MIT-I went quiet on the list and was withdrawn four months later in board minutes. **A wider corpus does not widen what the measurement is about.** **⚠ And the control is the whole lesson.** Running the new detector on the old window, purely to see whether it reproduced a known number, is the only reason the fold bug was found; the whole-archive figure looked entirely plausible on its own. **A census is not more trustworthy than a sample — it is bigger. | 231 | **A compression was assessed as having no impact, and the document that depended on the removed words was never opened.** The 1.0 → 1.1 pass compressed §2.5, the estoppel clause. `ACD-1.1-CHANGELIST.md` records it as *"§2.5 圧縮 —— estoppel の芯だけ残した"* and, in its risk table, as impact **無し** — none. **Measured 2026-09-25 by diffing 1.0 against the 1.2 draft sentence by sentence**: the pass removed two things. **(b)** *"That reliance is the purpose of this Dedication and not merely a foreseeable consequence of it"* — the element that separates **intended** reliance from mere foreseeability, and the stronger footing for promissory estoppel in the jurisdictions that draw that line. **(c)** *"and is estopped from doing so to the extent the law of any jurisdiction gives that principle effect"* — the explicit invocation. **And `ACD-1.0.comparison.md` answers a recorded criticism — *"The patent carve-out weakens estoppel defences"* — by quoting both of them verbatim as present features of the instrument.** They are present in 1.0 and absent from every successor. **The answer and the text it rests on had come apart, and the assessment that said so had not looked.** | **[CLOSED]** **Both restored to the 1.2 draft, and the cost is stated where the benefit is.** §2.5 now carries (b) and (c) again; the test applied was whether the words do work the remaining text does not. **⚠ What this says about the earlier assessment.** *"影響無し"* was not careless — the covenant does survive, and no recipient gains or loses an obligation. **What it missed is that impact is not only on recipients.** A dossier is part of what a reviewer reads, and a compression that empties one of its answers has an impact the clause-level reading does not see. **The question a compression has to answer is not only *what does the text still do* but *what still quotes the words being removed*.** **⚠ And the converse, which is real.** This was found by diffing whole versions rather than by reading either — the same sweep produced 47 differing sentences, of which **43 were rewording, three were documented drops, and one was this**. **A version diff is cheap and had never been run**; | 232 | **A pull request was closed as redundant on the strength of a comparison that checked whether the *fix* had landed, and never checked whether the *finding* had.** the pull request 1833 carried the sitemap licence contradiction. **and the binary observation** — the WebP's XMP was already correct on 2026-08-23, so the sweep of that day **reached metadata embedded inside a binary and missed one plain-text XML element.** **None of the six were in #222.** The closed PR's *code* was redundant; its *reading* was not. | **[CLOSED]** **All six salvaged into #222 as a dated addendum, keeping its history.** **⚠ The closing decision was not careless — it was measured, and it measured the wrong thing.** The comparison asked *is this change already in main*, and answered correctly: the sitemap line was fixed, 444h was implemented, the finding was registered. **What it never asked is whether the entry said anything the registered one did not** — and comparing two registers means reading both, which the file-level diff does not do. **⚠ The general form, which is about parallel work rather than about this PR.** When two sessions find the same defect, their *fixes* converge and their *readings* do not; closing on fix-equivalence discards whichever reading was second. **The cheap guard is the one the owner used**: before discarding, diff the abandoned branch's prose against what landed, not just its code. **⚠ And the converse.** Not closing it was not the alternative — it held a merge commit and could not be rebase-merged, and its `#143` text was the stale version that #229 has since replaced. **Closing was right; closing without extracting was the error**, and the two are separable. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 閉じた branch を main と突き合わせ、6 要素を #222 へ救出した。**合否**: **PR を重複として閉じる前に、その PR の*散文*が、残った側の散文が言っていないことを言っていないか確かめたか。** 修正の等価性は、読みの等価性を意味しない。 |
| 233 | **The thread our gap claim rests on had never been read, and reading it supplies the strongest argument against that claim being a gap at all.** One of the three gaps this instrument stands on is an express patent grant in a public-domain-style tool, and the dossier's warrant for it is that patents were *the* issue on which CC0 stalled at OSI. **Measured 2026-09-25 against the complete archive**: of 51 subject-matter threads with eight or more messages, **16 have never had a single line quoted anywhere in this directory**, and the largest of them is *"CC0 incompliant with OSD on patents"* — **69 messages, `license-review`, March 2012, the very argument the claim cites.** **Reading it confirms the warrant and hands us a counter we had never recorded.** Lawrence Rosen states the problem exactly as we do — *"There are FOSS-friendly software licensors who ... do not include an express patent grant (e.g., BSD). In some cases, they expressly **exclude** a patent grant (e.g., CC0)"* — and Carlo Piana argues the exclusion against the OSD directly. **But Russ Nelson answers with a symmetry we have never mentioned**: *"The BSD has no explicit patent waiver. ... your own logic demands that we deprecate the BSD OR that we give up on requiring patent waivers."* **If an express patent grant cannot be required without unseating BSD, then what we present as filling a gap is closer to a feature the list has declined to require** — and `implicit patent`, `deprecate the BSD` and `no explicit patent` return **zero hits** across `LICENSES/`. **And Nigel Tzeng describes CC0 in 2012 in the words we use for ourselves**: *"CC0 is first a public domain COPYRIGHT dedication with a permissive fallback"* — which is §3 plus §4, named on the list fourteen years before we wrote it. | **[CLOSED]** **The thread is preserved verbatim in `rounds/` and the counter is recorded rather than answered.** **⚠ This is #92's class at scale.** That entry found analysis in this directory resting on text the repository did not hold, and repaired eight instances. **The sweep that produced this one was mechanical** — every thread on our subject, against every line this directory has ever quoted — and it says the same thing about fifteen more threads. **Eight instances were a list; sixteen threads is a property.** **⚠ What the counter does and does not do.** It does not show the gap is unreal: Piana's objection stands in the same thread, and OSI's disposition of CC0 is what it is. **It shows that the list argued both sides in 2012 and we had only recorded one** — and a reviewer who knows this thread will supply Nelson's point whether or not we do. **Recording it first is the only version of this we control.** **⚠ And the converse about the method.** Two detectors disagreed on which threads were unmined — one scanned whole month files containing the subject line, the other only the messages of the thread — and the looser one reported matches drawn from unrelated threads in the same month. **The stricter count is the one used here.** A census of what we have *not* read is as instrument-dependent as a census of what we have. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 一次資料を `rounds/` へ無改変で保存し、反論を register へ入れた。**合否**: **ある論点を「これが争点だった」と述べるとき、その争点が起きた場所を読んだか。** 要約からの再構成は、反対側の議論を必ず落とす。 |
| 234 | **The check that exists so a local `verify` cannot be green while blind was blind to the directory this dossier lives in.** Check 434 stops `verify` reporting success while untracked files sit in governed directories, because the checks that matter most — mirror bijection, the tracked-file ban, the line ceiling — read `git ls-files` and cannot see what is not staged. **Its scope was `js/`, `e2e/`, `.github/scripts/` and `docs/`. `LICENSES/` was not in it**, and `LICENSES/` is where primary sources are added more often than anywhere else. **Measured the hard way on 2026-09-25**: a 3,712-line archive thread was written into `rounds/`, `npm run verify` returned **exit 0**, and CI then failed on **three checks at once** — 108 (no mirror), 365 (over the 1,000-line ceiling), 454 (over 800 lines and unbudgeted). **All three were invisible locally purely because the file was not yet staged.** | **[CLOSED]** **`LICENSES/` added to the scope, proved RED by putting an untracked file there.** **⚠ The gap is exactly the one the check was written to close, one directory over.** 434 was added because `git ls-files`-based checks are blind to untracked files; **it then enumerated the directories where that had already happened**, which is #194's shape — *a gate whose stated purpose names a risk, scanning a subset of the surfaces where the risk lives*. **⚠ And the incident cost nothing except the cycle, which is the argument for the check.** Nothing shipped; CI caught all three. **What it cost was a red CI on a PR whose diff was fine** — and the failure mode it prevents is the reverse, where the local green is trusted and the directory is one CI does not gate. **⚠ The converse worth stating.** Widening a scope is cheap here only because the four existing entries were already right; **the finding is not that someone chose badly but that the list was written from incidents rather than from the invariant.** The invariant is *every directory whose contents a `git ls-files` check reads*, and that is still an enumeration, not a derivation. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 射程へ `LICENSES/` を足し、未追跡ファイルを置いて RED を実測した。**合否**: **`git add` 前の `verify` は「緑」を意味しない。** 射程が列挙である限り、新しいディレクトリは常にその外側から始まる。 |
| 235 | **The nearest approved precedent for our patent gap was approved for a different reason than ours, and the reason is only in the review thread — not in the licence text we census.** `BSD-2-Clause-Patent` is an OSI-approved permissive licence that adds an express patent grant, which is the shape of the gap Section 8 exists to fill — **one of three** (UPL-1.0 and BlueOak-1.0.0 have the same shape; **corrected 2026-09-25**: this entry first said *"the one"*, which the census it cites contradicts), and **the one whose review thread we have read**. **`ACD-1.0.gap-census.md` already carries it** — the census reads the 141 approved, non-deprecated texts of SPDX 3.29.0 (**corrected 2026-09-25** from *"149"*, which is the 3.28.0 count including deprecated IDs) and correctly reports that the clause exists there. **What a text census cannot report is why the list approved it**, and the 2016 review thread says so in the submitter's own words: *"the desire of certain organizations to have a simple permissive license that is compatible with the GNU General Public License (GPL), version 2, but which also has an express patent grant included."* **That is not our reason.** Ours is a public-domain-style instrument reaching machine learning and text-and-data mining; theirs was GPLv2 compatibility for a permissive base. **The two gaps are adjacent and distinct, and the distinction is only visible in the thread.** **The thread also supplies a submission form we can be measured against** — an approved submission stated a **RATIONALE as a problem with existing licences**, proposed its own **PROLIFERATION CATEGORY** (*"Special Purpose License, or, alternatively, as an approved variant of the BSD 2-clause license"*), and carried a **DISTINGUISHING FROM OTHER OSI-APPROVED LICENSES** section. **Our packet declines to propose a tag** (*"None proposed"*), which is compliant — the process *invites* rather than requires one — **but it is a difference from the approved form, and it is recorded here rather than discovered by a reviewer.** | **[CLOSED]** **The thread is preserved verbatim in `rounds/` (ten parts) and the rationale is recorded where the census cannot reach.** **⚠ The strongest thing in the thread is a failure mode we avoid, and the reason we avoid it is worth naming.** Richard Fontana warned the submitter that reusing Apache-2.0's patent language creates a mismatch — the grant is made by *"each copyright holder and contributor"* while the clause scoping the claims *"assumes that anyone granting the patent license is a copyright holder."* **Section 8.1 cannot have that defect**, because the definition of *Dedicator* — **§1.3 in ACD-1.0, renumbered to §1.4 in the 1.2 draft** — makes it *each* person applying the Dedication and says the instrument *"operates severally as to each"*, so grantor and claim-owner are the same party distributively. **That is structural, not lucky** — but it holds only when the definition and Section 8.1 are read together, which is #157's axis. **⚠ And writing that sentence tripped the class this entry is about.** The first draft cited *Section 1.4* alone; **Check 470 rejected it, because the definition is Section 1.3 in the submission target.** #144 is the entry recording that clause numbers are not preserved across versions, and the sentence that fell to it was in an entry about reading a precedent properly. **A version-scoped number is a claim about which text you are describing**, and the safety net caught it before a reviewer did. **⚠ What this does not do.** It does not make the gap larger or smaller. `gap-census.md` measured the texts and found the clause exists in three permissive licences; **this adds why one of them was approved, which is evidence about the list rather than about the texts.** **⚠ And the converse on method.** The first extraction of this thread pulled **72 messages including four from unrelated threads**, because it matched the licence name in message *bodies*. Matching on the unfolded `Subject:` gives **68 from a single month and a single list**. **A thread is defined by its subject header, not by where its name appears** — and the looser extraction would have put four foreign messages into `rounds/`, which is the one directory whose entire value is that its contents are what they say they are. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 一次資料を `rounds/` へ 10 部で保存し、承認理由と提出の形を register へ入れた。**合否**: **承認済みの隣人について述べるとき、その条文だけを読んだのか、承認された理由まで読んだのか。** テキストの census は前者しか答えない。 |
| 236 | **Four people have said on these lists that an express patent grant is not required, one of them that silence already carries an implicit grant by estoppel, and this dossier had recorded none of them.** The remaining eleven unmined subject threads were read in full at the owner's request — 171 messages. **Four turned out not to be on our subject at all** (a filter matching *model*, *patent* and *public domain* pulled in a thread about source-code obfuscation, one about a Caps Lock patent, one about IETF's RAND policy, and one about liability insurance): **the census of what we have not read over-selected by 36%.** The other seven carried the following. **(a) The patent gap is weighed against us by four independent voices.** Matthew Garrett, 2005: *"The OSD doesn't require that a license give you a patent grant in the first place."* John Cowan, 2014: *"nobody can say that licenses that don't grant patent rights explicitly are not open-source licenses."* Russ Nelson, 2012 (#233). **And Bruce Perens, 2005, goes further**: *"There is an implicit patent grant in the BSD license. If you distribute software embodying your own patents with a license to make use of the software, you are effectively granting an estoppel regarding those patents."* **If that is right, the silence our Section 8 exists to cure may not be silence.** **(b) The estoppel our Section 2.5 builds was attacked on this list in 2004, on the element we restored today.** Daniel Wallace set out promissory estoppel's four elements; **John Cowan objected on element two** — *"When I license my work, I don't **know** that you rely on my promises, since I don't know you at all"* — and **Lawrence Rosen rejected the frame outright**: *"Promissory estoppel is a contract doctrine that has nothing to do with a bare license like the GPL."* **The sentence restored to Section 2.5 hours earlier (E34) is the answer to Cowan's objection** — reliance as *purpose* rather than foreseeable consequence is exactly element two — **and we restored it without knowing the objection existed.** Rosen's is not answered. **(c) The head of OSI said in terms that CC0 was not refused.** Simon Phipps, 2014: *"We did not decide against CC0. ... Creative Commons withdrew it from the approval process, but that's what happened, **not an OSI denial**."* `review-corpus.md` already carried that correction from the thread's subject line; **`comparison.md` §1.4 still heads the section *"the only precedent of our type that stalled at OSI"***. (**Resolved the same day, 2026-09-25 19:32 JST, commit `138f3885`**: the heading now reads *「否決されずに取り下げられた先例」* — withdrawn, not denied. **The sentence above is kept as written; its present tense was true when written and is not now.**) **And Richard Fontana, in the same thread**: *"I believe CC0 is obviously lowercase-o, lowercase-s open source despite the clause about patents."* **(d) Section 3's central question was argued in 2004 and answered against it.** Arnoud Engelfriet: moral rights *"cannot be transferred or given up"* in most countries and permit the author to act *"even when saying 'this work is in the public domain'"* — and therefore *"it may be impossible for a work to truly enter the public domain in any other way"* than expiry. **That is Section 12's problem stated twenty-two years before we wrote Section 12.** **(e) And one thing runs our way, as an arc rather than a quote.** Intel's 2001 BSD+Patent tied its patent grant to GPL-licensed operating systems and was argued down on OSD 3, 6 and 8 — Cowan: *"this license is in effect specific to Linux"* — **while the 2016 version, whose grant is unconditional, was approved** (#235). **ACD Section 8.2 makes its grants *"subject to no condition"* and not terminable on any ground, with the absence of retaliation stated as deliberate.** | **[CLOSED]** **All seven relevant threads preserved verbatim in `rounds/` (eleven files), and the counters are recorded rather than answered.** **⚠ (a) is the heaviest and it is not new in kind — it is new in weight.** #233 recorded one voice; this makes four, spanning 2005 to 2014, none of them ours to rebut. **The honest position is not that the gap is unreal but that the list has repeatedly declined to treat the absence of a patent grant as disqualifying**, and a reviewer who knows these threads will say so. Perens's estoppel point is the one that could make Section 8 redundant rather than merely unrequired; **it is also a 2005 remark, not a holding, and no court has said it.** **⚠ (b) cuts both ways and the favourable half must not be overstated.** That the restored sentence answers Cowan is a fact about the text, not evidence that estoppel works; **Rosen's objection — that the doctrine does not reach a bare licence at all — would defeat Section 2.5 entirely if a court agreed, and nothing here shows one would.** **⚠ (c) was a live inconsistency in our own directory when this entry was written** (resolved by `138f3885`, see above), which #222 is the entry about: one document corrected the CC0 characterisation and another kept the superseded heading. **⚠ And the method's own result.** Reading eleven threads produced findings in seven and nothing in four, and **the four were selected by a keyword filter that could not tell a machine-learning model from a model code of conduct.** A census of the unread is only as good as the topic test, and this one was measured after the fact rather than before. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 11 スレッド全件を読み、関係する 7 件を保存し、反論 4 件を register へ入れた。**合否**: **我々の gap を弱める発言を、我々が先に書いているか。** 4 人がリスト上で述べていたことを、我々は 1 件も記録していなかった。 |
| 237 | **理事会が実際に不承認を議決した、我々の時代にいちばん近い審査の理由は OSD 違反ではなく「主題」だった。そしてその理由が名指しした語が、ACD-1.0 §1.2 の列挙に在る。** Mulan Open Works Licenses 4 本は 2023-02-20 に `license-review` へ提出され、**2023-09-15 に理事会が不承認を議決した**。委員会の勧告文（Chestek 氏が 2023-09-13 にリストへ投稿）はこう述べる —— *"Resolved that it is the opinion of the OSI that the licenses, **as open culture licenses, are not appropriate for OSI approval**."* 理由は本文の欠陥ではなく、**ライセンスが対象と定義した material の列挙**である —— *"The subject matter of licenses is 'intellectual achievement protected by copyright law ... including but not limited to a written work, a musical work, a fine art work, an architecture work, a photographic work, **an audiovisual work**, a graphic work, and a model work.' Because of the differences in the purposes and goals of these different (albeit related) areas, **open culture licenses are outside the purview of the Open Source Initiative**."* **同じスレッドで委員長は境界も述べている** —— *"the OSI is not in the practice of approving licenses that are not software-specific. ... Sometimes an OSI license will **cross over into data or hardware when tied to software**, but not a license altogether unrelated to software."*（Chestek 氏 2023-08-10）。Piana 氏は *"In general, **all the licenses should be rejected for not being (Open Source) software licenses**."*（2023-07-05）、Berkus 氏は *"OSI has **not previously approved content licenses** ... it would be an **organizational policy change** and therefore not a routine license approval."*（2023-03-01）。**ACD-1.0 §1.2 はこう定義する** —— *"'Work' means the material to which the Dedicator has applied this Dedication, in whole or in part, in any medium and in any form, including source code, object code, documentation, data, metadata, **audiovisual material**, and any collection or compilation of these."* **不承認理由が名指しした語の 1 つが、我々の定義にそのまま在る。** | **[CLOSED]** **記録し、提出パケットで先に自分から名指しした**（`submission.md` §B.0）。**⚠ 差は実在し、それを過小に言わない。** MulanOWL の定義には**ソフトウェアが 1 語も無い**のに対し、§1.2 は **source code / object code から始まり**、PREAMBLE の第 1 文は *"Software and the works that surround it"* である。**Chestek 氏自身の carve-out —— *"cross over into data or hardware **when tied to software**"* —— は ACD の形をそのまま述べている。** だが**それは我々の読みであって委員会の判断ではない**。危険は構造ではなく**読まれ方**にある: §1.2 を単独で読む審査者にとって、この列挙は open culture ライセンスの定義と見分けがつかない。**そして本文は凍結中なので、この増分で打てる手は「先に言う」ことだけだった。** **⚠ 逆側も同じ重さで**: 列挙を狭める案（"audiovisual material" を落とす）は **gap 主張を自分で折る** —— 本 instrument が名乗る用途は「学習されるための著作物」で、データセットと学習済みモデルはまさに非コード資産である。**主題の広さは副作用ではなく設計であり、それを隠すのは候補の改善ではなく開示の後退である。** **合否**: **不承認の理由として実際に使われた語が我々の定義に在ることを、審査者より先に我々が書いているか。** |

**§8 は「要求されている」のではなく「安全である」**。**§8 が担っているのは適合ではなく gap 主張**であり、この 3 段はその区別を強めこそすれ、gap を証明しない。**⚠ (2) の引用は、我々の別の訂正を第 2 の出典で裏づけてもいる。** #222 / #236(c) が直した「CC0 は否決されていない」は Phipps I think it's safe to say that **there are some previously approved licenses that would not be approved if submitted today** based on that additional experience."* **同じスレッドに 2 件。** John Cowan 氏 —— *"**We are reluctant to go through the effort of approving licenses which no one but the drafter of the license will ever make use of.**"*（B2 = 採用実績ゼロ、2006 年の姿）。**そして同年 11 月、Rosen 氏が特許条項そのものに向けて proliferation を述べている** —— *"**I don't think there's much enthusiasm to come up with yet another way to say these things about patents.**"*（BiOS スレッド 2006-11-15） | **[CLOSED]** **3 件とも記録した。****⚠ Dillard 氏の一文が最も重い。** 我々は `comparison.md` と提出パケットで **Unlicense（2020 承認）を「同型で承認された先例」として使っている**。この一文は、**承認済みであることが現在の基準を教えない**と述べている ——しかも Unlicense について我々自身が「起草が粗いと広く合意されつつ承認された」と書いており、**それはまさに「今なら通らないかもしれない承認」の記述である。** **逆側**: これは 2006 年の一参加者の発言であって規則ではなく、**我々が Unlicense を引くのは「承認されたのだから我々も通る」ではなく「献呈 + 許諾の併置という形が審査対象になりうる」ことを示すため**である（`submission.md` §1b）。**その用途なら Dillard 氏の一文に当たらない** —— 当たるのは、先例を**合否の予測**に使うときだけである。**そこで用途を提出パケットの側で明示した。** **⚠ Rosen 氏の proliferation は §8.4 に直接当たり、答えは 1 つしかない** —— 既存の特許許諾条項は *"the Work and Derivative Works"* で止まり、**学習済みモデルと出力に届かない**。届かないことを示せなければ、§8.4 は「特許について言う、もう一つの言い方」である。**この主張は本ドシエに既に在るが、反対の形で名前がついたのは本増分が初めてである。** **合否**: **先例を予測に使っていないか。gap を「新しい言い方」と区別できているか。** |
| 240 | **件名フィルタの precision は測ったが recall は測っていなかった。測ったら 4 スレッド漏れていた —— そのうち最も重要な 1 件は、件名が固有名詞だけでできていた。** #236 は「未読 11 スレッドのうち 4 件は主題外だった = 36% の過選択」と**自分の検出器の誤検出率**を測った。**取りこぼし率は測っていない。** そこで逆を測った —— **件名に主題語を持たないが、本文では主題を実質的に論じており、かつドシエが一度も引いていないスレッド**。**4 件出た**: `For Approval: MulanOWL BY v1; ...`（2023-02〜10・14 通）、`Biological Open Source`（2006-11・11 通）、`For Approval: Broad Institute Public License (BIPL)`（2006-07・28 通）、`OSI enforcement? (Was Re: Microsoft use of the term "Open Source")`（2008-01・重複除去後 124 通）。**最も価値が高かったのは MulanOWL（#237 / #238 の出どころ）で、その件名が主題語を持たない理由は単純である —— 件名がライセンスの名前でできているから。** | **[CLOSED]** **4 件すべてを全通読み、3 件を `rounds/` に保存した**（20 file・行境界分割・結合で byte 一致を生成時に検証）。**一般形: 件名フィルタは固有名詞に盲目である。** 我々が知らないライセンス名は主題語にならないので、**「まだ読んでいないものの census」は、知らないものを構造的に数え落とす。** **⚠ そしてこの recall テスト自体が同じ盲点を 1 段下に持つ。** 判定は本文の主題語によるので、**件名が固有名詞で、かつ本文も我々の語彙を使わないスレッド**は依然として見えない。**「余す事無く読んだ」と書けるのは、この 2 つの検出器が届く範囲についてだけである。** **⚠ 4 件目は読み切って、還元がほぼゼロだった。** 2008-01 の 124 通は商標と用語の論争で、**立場を動かす材料は 1 件も出ていない**（唯一近いのは、公有化献呈が著作者性の立証手段を失わせるという Verdy 氏の指摘と、それに対する Moen 氏の *"Attaching a copyright notice while attempting to dedicate a work to the public domain seems at best incongruous"* で、**どちらも審査者の立場ではない**）。**保存もしていない** —— 引用して立場を動かしたものだけを `rounds/` に置く規約に従い、代わりに**再取得できる座標**（`license-discuss` 2008-January・上記件名）をここに書いた。**recall テストは本物の取りこぼしと、本物の空振りの両方を出す。空振りを報告しないと、次の担当者は同じ 124 通を読む。** **合否**: **「全部読んだ」の範囲を、検出器の届く範囲として書いているか。** |
| 241 | **A census of every approved licence found four precedents that narrow our gap claims, and none of them had been discussed anywhere in this dossier.** Measured 2026-09-25 against all 141 OSI-approved, non-deprecated texts of SPDX 3.29.0 (`ACD-1.0.gap-census.md`, generated by `.github/scripts/generate_gap_census.py`). **(1) CERN-OHL-P/S/W-2.0** grant patents over *"Products"*, defined as *"any device, component, work or physical object … arising from the use, application or processing of Covered Source"* — **a reading on which a model trained on Covered Source is a Product is open**, so *"no approved licence grants patents that reach a trained model"* cannot be asserted; what holds is that none **names** models, parameters or outputs. **(2) UPL-1.0, BlueOak-1.0.0 and BSD-2-Clause-Patent** already combine a permissive grant with an express patent grant under one notice condition; BlueOak's grant is unlimited by activity (*"everything with this software"*), so training is covered in terms. **(3) EUPL-1.1/1.2** already contain the structure of Section 12: *"In the countries where moral rights apply, the Licensor waives his right to exercise his moral right to the extent allowed by law"*. **(4) WordNet** grants *"Permission to use, copy, modify and distribute this software and database"* — a database as licensed material, though no right is named. | **[CLOSED]** **Recorded where a reviewer meets it**: `submission-reference.md` §2 now carries CERN-OHL-P-2.0, the UPL / BlueOak / BSD-2-Clause-Patent table with what it concedes, WordNet, and EUPL on the moral-rights row; `REVIEWERS.md` narrows the patent limb to *"names models"*; `comparison.md`'s table no longer says the Unlicense and CC0 are silent on successors (both say their dedication binds *"heirs and successors"*). **What survives on every row is the same thing: the condition.** Each precedent keeps at least one; ACD-1.0 keeps none (4.3, 10.1). **A reviewer who thinks a single notice condition is not worth a new licence has answered against us, and that answer is fair** (#84). **⚠ What this does not do**: it does not measure effect. Absence of a word is not absence of a right, and presence of a broad word (*work*, *everything*) is not a holding that it reaches a model. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 全数で当て、最も近い反例を提出参考資料に先に置き、gap の主張を実測で立つ範囲へ狭めた。**合否**: **既存ライセンスが X を持たないと書くとき、全数で当てたか、選んだ比較対象で当てたか。** |
| 242 | **我々は「記録に在る唯一の日本発の提出」と書いた。2 件目が在り、しかも承認されている —— そして提出したのは弁護士だった。** `review-precedents.md` §1.105（2026-09-22）は TOPPERS License（2015）について *"記録に在る唯一の日本発の提出であり、我々は一度も開いていなかった"* と述べている。**誤りである。** **IPA Font License v1.0** は 2009-02-04 に `license-review` へ提出され、2009-03 に Michael Tiemann 氏が *"I hereby add my own affirmation that this license appears to meet the criteria of the OSD in letter and in spirit"*、Bruce Perens 氏が *"the major issues have been resolved"*、Chuck Swiger 氏が *"the license seems to be fully OSD-compliant"* と述べ、**承認された**。**提出者は `mhmjapan.com` の Yuko Noguchi 氏 —— 森・濱田松本法律事務所の弁護士**で、**独立行政法人情報処理推進機構（IPA）を代理している**。さらに 2025-09 に `license-discuss` で後日談（WOFF 変換版の配布停止要請）が論じられ、**Shuji Sado 氏（Open Source Group Japan 会長）が「ライセンスの資格の問題ではなく運用上の compliance の問題だ」と答えている** ——**ドシエは佐渡氏を 9 file で引用しながら、このスレッドを引いていなかった。** | **[CLOSED]** **§1.105 の「唯一」を撤回し、本スレッドを `rounds/` に逐語保存して §1.110 に読みを書いた。** **⚠ 訂正の向きは一方向ではない。** **有利**: 日本発の提出は失敗例 1 件ではなく、**承認された先例が 2009 年に在る**。**不利**: **その承認された先例には弁護士が付いていた** —— 大手法律事務所の弁護士が政府機関を代理して提出し、リストの指摘に応じて**本文を 2 度書き直している**。**B1（法的レビューが無い）は、日本の先例を並べると鮮明になる** ——弁護士が付いた方が通り、付かなかった方（TOPPERS）は翻訳の証明で止まった。**⚠ そして「唯一」と書いた原因は探し方にある。** §1.105 は**差出人のドメインから日本の参加者を数えて**辿り着いた。**その方法では、日本の組織を代理する外国法域の弁護士も、日本の組織名しか出てこない提出も見えない** ——IPA Font の提出者は `mhmjapan.com` で日本のドメインだが、**スレッドを特定したのは差出人ではなく件名（提出名）の側だった。** **絶対量化子を書くときは、その前に検出器を 2 つ通すこと。** **合否**: **「唯一」「初めて」「すべて」と書いた文が、1 つの検出器の出力だけに依拠していないか。** |
| 243 | **「まだ読んでいないもの」を主題語で数えていた。提出名で数え直したら、ドシエが一度も言及していない提出が 96 件あった。** **⚠ #241 と混同しないこと** —— あちらは**承認済みライセンスの本文** 141 本を我々の gap 主張に当てた census で、こちらは**アーカイブ上の提出名** 146 件をドシエの言及に当てた census である。**対象が違い（本文 / 議論）、答える問いも違う（gap は残っているか / 我々は何を読んでいないか）。** #240 は件名フィルタが**固有名詞に盲目**だと記録した。ならば**固有名詞そのもので数えればよい** —— 506 か月のアーカイブから `For Approval:` / `For Discussion:` / `Approval Request:` 形式の件名を全部抜き、ライセンス名に正規化すると **146 件の異なる提出名**が得られる。**うち 96 件をドシエは一度も言及していない**（照合は stopword を落とした中核語の 2 語連鎖・対照 3 件で検証）。通数の上位から: Microsoft Permissive License（2005-12・274 通）/ License Zero Reciprocal（2017・141 + 47 通）/ Convertible Free Software License（2018-12・129 通）/ Transitive Grace Period Public Licence（2008-12・128 通）/ Open Source Software Alliance License（2003-09・122 通）/ **MXM Public License（2009-04・41 通）** / **IPA Font License（2009-02・37 通）**。 | **[OPEN]** **本増分で閉じたのは 1 件だけである**（IPA Font → #242 / §1.110）。**残る 95 件は読んでいない。** **⚠ この数を「未読 96 件」と読んではならない。** 検出器は**件名から抽出した提出名**を**ドシエの本文**へ 2 語連鎖で照合しており、**OpenMDW は「未言及」と出たが実際には 21 file で論じられている**（我々は略称で書き、件名は *"OpenMDW License Agreement"* だった）。**上位 23 件を手で grep して確かめたところ 21 件は本当に未言及だったが、この照合は候補リストであって census ではない** —— #87 が *"Name matching is a floor, not a census"* と書いたのと同じ制約が、名前の側から数えても消えない。**⚠ 優先順位は通数ではない。** いちばん重いのは **MXM Public License** で、理由は通数ではなく**指し先**である —— Carlo Piana 氏が 2023 年に *"we have in the past discussed many times (see discussion of **CC0 or the MXM license or the W3C license**) pure copyright licenses excluding all other rights"* と述べており、**現役の委員が、我々の特許除外の論点の先例として名指ししている 3 件のうち 2 件を、我々は読んでいない。** **潰し方**: 通数順ではなく**「委員が名指ししたもの」→「我々の類型に形が近いもの」→「通数」**の順に読む。**合否**: **未読の在庫を、通数ではなく我々の論点への近さで並べているか。** |

It applies equally to all of those"* である。**ドシエは人格権を「gap か mission creep か」の分岐として記録してきた**（bottleneck B8・`against.md` #55 系）。**OSD の起草者の読みでは、人格権は OSD が既に射程に入れている制限の 1 つであり、分岐の片側（mission creep）は成り立ちにくくなる。** | **[CLOSED]** **記録した。** **有利**: §12 は人格権を放棄または不行使とするので、**OSD が制限とみなす手段を 1 つ潰している。** §1.59 の「日本では著作権のみを述べるライセンスは改変を保証できない」（Open Source Group Japan 会長の 2024 年の主張）と合わせると、**§12 は「余計なことをしている」のではなく「OSD が要求する自由を droit d'auteur 法域で実際に届かせるための条」と読める。** **⚠ 逆側は 2 つある。** **(1)** これはリスト上の 1 発言であって、OSI の判断でも規則でもない。**起草者の読みは権威だが、承認は委員会と理事会が行う**（`board-decisions.md` の *"prior approval does not bind"*）。**(2) そして有利に読みすぎると B8 のもう半分が消える** —— §12 への実際の批判は「OSD 違反だ」ではなく **「人格権は licensable でない」**（Piana 氏・#104）と **「1 つの instrument に 1 つの権利」**（Landley 氏・#102）で、**本発言はそのどちらにも答えていない。** **合否**: **「OSD が人格権を見ている」と「§12 が有効である」を混ぜていないか。** |

The adverse list has grown from 14 entries to **247** across ten rounds of examination. When this
section was written it had not grown at all, and that asymmetry was itself a distortion: a document whose adverse side
scales while its surviving side is frozen is not more honest, it is differently inaccurate. What
follows is what actually held, restricted to things that were tested rather than asserted.

1. **No design decision has been overturned.** Eight rounds of adversarial reading — four
   prompted by third parties, four self-initiated — have produced **247 adverse facts, 13
   errata, and five corrections to our own documents** (three descriptions that were wrong:
   #24, #35, #36; and two gaps in the dossier's own machinery: #34, #40). **None of them changed a design decision recorded in
   `docs/architecture/acd-license-rationale.md`.** What changed was the accuracy of later
   explanations of those decisions (#24) and the precision of the text's wording (E1–E10). For an
   instrument with no legal review, "the design held and the drafting was imprecise" is a
   meaningfully better result than the reverse.
2. **Nothing found changes what a recipient may do.** Every errata entry is a matter of
   precision, scope of wording, or the form in which a conclusion is stated. A defect of the
   other kind would be recorded here immediately, and the honest response to one might be
   withdrawal rather than repair.
3. **The machine-readable layer resolves, and its one gap is recorded.** All **33** clause
   pointers in `ACD-1.0.machine.json` resolve to clauses that exist and are about the subject
   claimed, and both machine-readable files record `osiApproved: false` and `spdxListed: false`
   rather than overstating status. That is the same class of error as #24, checked mechanically
   and absent. **What is not clean is selection**: the descriptor enumerates six things the
   instrument does not do and omits the one thing it does (#59), so this point claims accuracy,
   not completeness. The pointer count is checked on every CI run against the descriptor itself
   (Check 460 face (i)), which is how the figure "29" written here was found to be wrong.
4. **The question it answers is not the question the other two answer.** OpenMDW and ModelGo
   licence model artefacts; ACD-1.0 addresses computational use of any work, including material
   whose authorship may not subsist at all (§9). Neither of them has anything to say about that
   last case. This does not settle proliferation — see #28 and #33 — but it does settle overlap.
5. **The sections that no amendment closes are the ones the design turns on.** Most of the
   distinguishing features could be added to an existing licence as clauses; the split is
   enumerated in `comparison.md` §1.35 and is checked against its own two lists on every CI run
   (Check 460 face (j)), so the arithmetic is not repeated here — **this passage previously said
   "four of six" and "the remaining two" while the lists held four and three** (#65). The ones
   that could not be added — §9's refusal to presume that a right subsists, §10.1's absence of
   any condition, and computational use being defined once at the top rather than bolted on at
   the edge — are not features but consequences of where the instrument starts. That is a
   narrower claim than "it is different", and it is the one that survives the incumbents moving.
6. **The clause-level defects found so far are all of one kind.** Every errata entry, every
   description corrected in our own documents, and every adverse fact has produced **no defect
   that changes what a recipient may do.** Each is a matter of precision, of scope of wording, or
   of a conclusion stated in the wrong form. **The counts are deliberately not restated here** —
   `errata.md` and the list above are the authority, and a number written into prose is stale the
   day after (this passage previously read "five errata … forty-five adverse facts" against
   actuals of ten and sixty-nine, **and claimed in the same breath that Check 460 enforced them,
   which it did not** — #70). That is not proof of quality; it is a statement about the kind of
   fault that has been found, and the kind that has not.
7. **A first approval in this category would help, whoever earns it.** #33 records that no
   AI-specific licence has ever been approved as an adverse fact, and it is one. The same fact
   read forward is different: **if either instrument now in review is approved, the barrier that
   #33 describes stops existing for everyone**, including a submission that does not compete
   with them. Being early in a category with no precedent is a cost and a position at once, and
   recording only the cost was part of the asymmetry corrected here.

### What is deliberately not argued to reviewers

That an individual, without counsel or an institution, drafted and pursued an instrument of this
kind is a real fact about this project. **It is not an argument for approval and will not be
offered as one.** Reviewers assess the licence, not the circumstances of the person submitting
it, and asking them to weigh those circumstances would invite exactly the discount that #5 (AI
drafting) and #1 (no legal review) already earn honestly. The fact belongs in the repository's
own account of what it is; it does not belong in the submission.

## What would cause withdrawal

Stated in advance so that it is not a reaction to how the discussion goes:

- If the list's view is that §4's independent grant does not make this a licence, then it is not a
  candidate in its present form, and the answer will be to say so rather than to redraft under
  pressure.
- If a defect is found in the text, it will be **reported and left in place** while the
  discussion is open. The text is frozen and CI pins its SHA-256 (Check 453); changing wording
  underneath a live review would make that review meaningless.
- `ACD-1.0.submission.md` §E.2 records the same conditions in the packet prepared for `license-review`.
- `LICENSES/rounds/2026-08-26-license-discuss-sent.txt` is the message that was actually sent to
  `license-discuss`, reproduced verbatim from the public archive.

| 134 | **A reviewer's central question about the nearest contemporary licence is one our text answers only partly: "How do you know what the covered work is?"** Richard Fontana put it to the OpenMDW steward on 2026-08-16, and returned to it through a month of review. ACD-1.0 defines the Work by **the act of applying the text** (§1.2: *"the material to which the Dedicator has applied this Dedication"*), says the Dedication *"reaches only rights the Dedicator holds"* (§2.7), and places on the Dedicator a duty to identify material owned by others *"in a form that accompanies the Work"* (§2.6) | **[CLOSED]** **Recorded, then narrowed the same day after reading §16 (see the end of this entry).** What the licence guarantees is that **nothing is covered which the Dedicator does not hold**, and that **anything excluded will be identified**; what it does not do is tell a third party, from the text alone, **which files in a given tree carry it**. In this repository that is machine-determinable — an SPDX tag, `ACD-1.0.machine.json`, the `.well-known` declaration and a root `LICENSE` all say so — **but that is a property of this adopter, not of the licence**, and ACD-1.0 is meant to be adoptable without editing (§4b). **Two things keep it from being a defect rather than a limit**: every widely approved permissive licence has the same property (MIT does not say which files it covers either), and §6.5 states inside the text why machine-determinability matters, which is more than the incumbents do. **What we will not claim**: that §2.6 makes scope determinable in general. It makes *exclusions* identifiable, which is narrower. **Correction, same day**: the first version of this entry said the answer *"leans on practice rather than on text"*. **That was too strong, and I wrote it before reading §16.** §16.1 gives a notice form that **includes an SPDX tag**, and **§16.2 states that the identifier alone is sufficient notice, whether or not a copy of the text accompanies the work**. **So the text does supply a machine-readable marker for scope; it is not silent.** **What is unchanged, and is the actual limit**: the text **supplies** a marker but does not **require** per-file marking, so a tree with no markers is still indeterminate from the text alone — the property MIT and every approved permissive licence share. **The process failure is worth one line**: I characterised a clause family before reading it, which is the same shape as claiming something is absent without grepping (#88, #92). Worked in `ACD-1.0.review-corpus.md` §1.78 |

| 138 | **The prepared answer to the steward-continuity question credits Section 16.4 with a guarantee that Section 16.4 does not give — and we had already recorded the gap ourselves.** Q12 of `ACD-1.0.review-responses-meta.md` is the answer to the single-steward objection (register **B12**), and its first pillar reads: *"Section 16.4 makes the text of ACD-1.0 immutable: it may be copied and distributed verbatim only, and any change is a different version under a different identifier. So there is no maintenance activity that a user depends on."* **Two things in that sentence are checkable against the frozen text and both fail.** (i) *"verbatim only"* is not what the clause says: its first sentence permits verbatim copying, its second forbids modified distribution **under the name or the identifier**, and its **third expressly permits** a modified text *"under a different name"*. A reviewer with §16.4 open sees the third sentence at a glance. (ii) *"any change is a different version under a different identifier"* is **exactly what E14 records §16.4 failing to secure** — the clause names only `"ACD-1.0"`, so a third party distributing a modified text as `ACD-1.1` or `ACD-2.0` is outside its wording, and we assert no trademark (§11.1). **We found that hole on 2026-09-10 and left the answer that depends on it untouched.** The same over-attribution stands in two further places (`faq.md` B4: *"改訂は必ず別の識別子になる"*; `review-responses.md` §7 steward row) | **[EXTERNAL]** **Corrected on 2026-09-13 in all three places, and the limitation is now carried in the answer rather than only in the errata.** What actually secures each part is now stated separately: **the identifier denoting one fixed text** is §16.4's purpose clause and holds for `ACD-1.0`; **the steward not revising 1.0** is `FROZEN.md` policy enforced by Check 453's sha256 pin, not a clause effect; **successor identifiers** are secured by neither, which is E14 and is why registration matters (B11). **The argument survives the correction** — Q12's claim is that *a user depends on no maintenance activity*, and that rests on §10.4 (no condition, no termination) plus the pin, neither of which E14 touches. **What does not survive is the phrasing.** The general form is the one this dossier keeps meeting from a new side: **we record a defect in the file that collects defects, and leave the argument that relies on the defect standing elsewhere.** #66 / #67 named the reader-per-face version of this; here the two faces are *the errata list* and *the answer a reviewer receives*, and only the second one is sent **潰し方**: **§16.4 が与えられない保証は、外の仕組み（SPDX / OSI の登録簿）が与える。** 我々にできるのは **(a) 何が何を担保しているかを分けて述べること**（識別子の指示対象は §16.4、steward の継承は §16.5、改変の検知は Check 453）、**(b) 担保していないものを担保していないと書くこと**で、どちらも実行済み。**#216 が示したとおり、この entry の教訓（欠陥を記録した file に依拠する主張を直していなかった）は再発しうる。** |
| 139 | **Every outcome this dossier recorded was read from a source that does not record outcomes.** Until 2026-09-13 the entire "what happens to submissions like ours" dataset — the approved/rejected split, the time-to-decision median, the base rate of silence — was derived from the `license-review` archive, i.e. **from the discussion**. The archive announces some decisions and not others: §1.69 recorded that only 8 of 21 submission threads carried an announcement and called the other 13 "unknown". **They were not unknown; they were in a different document.** OSI publishes board minutes, in which every approval, rejection and withdrawal appears as a motion with a proposer and a second. **The concrete cost is visible in §1.47**: this dossier read the MIT-I thread, saw it stop in 2025-08, and wrote *"the submitter did not withdraw it — the thread simply died"*. The minutes of 2025-12-19 record that **the committee withdrew it**, four months later. **Our sentence was a correct description of the archive and a wrong description of the world** | **[CLOSED]** **Corrected in §1.47, and the source is now in the dossier** (`ACD-1.0.board-decisions.md`, with the verbatim minutes in `rounds/`). **The general form is what matters, and it cuts against a reading we have leaned on**: #109 and #117 measured base rates of silence to argue that our own silence is unremarkable. **Those measurements are of threads, and a silent thread can still have had an outcome recorded elsewhere** — so "no reply" was never evidence of "no decision", and we had not checked. **Withdrawal is also not rejection**: the same minutes withdrew the NIST licence, which was then **approved** on 2026-02-20. **The failure is not that we read the archive badly; it is that we asked one question of one source and never asked which source records the answer.** |
| 140 | **The dossier's discipline of recording adverse facts has a matching failure mode that nothing in it guards against, and it fired today.** `BLIND-SPOTS.md` already records that *keeping a list of adverse facts creates pressure to err on the adverse side* (#87, #131 — the discipline says *do not inflate in our favour* and has never said *do not inflate against ourselves*). **What was missing is the operational form of that pressure: a finding gets written up, the adverse reading is developed, and the favourable reading is not looked for at all.** Measured on the day's own output: the board's three sentences on *"not approved"* were written up as landing on us, and **two favourable readings were absent** — that a holding status carries no finding of OSD non-compliance, which is the one line that would most damage a dossier whose posture is *check us*; and that the board narrowed rejection to *"only if it violates the OSD"*. **Neither had been considered, let alone rejected.** The same omission is visible structurally: **3 of 13 entries in the bottleneck register stated the cost of the defect and not the cost of curing it** | **[EXTERNAL]** **Both sides now written, and the lens is a standing dimension** (`BLIND-SPOTS.md`), added at the owner's request on the same day (*"everything has two sides"*). **The result vindicates the method rather than the optimism**: looking for the favourable reading produced two, **and then produced the record that destroys one of them** — Project Tick GPL was rejected in 2026-02 because *"ambiguities in drafting make it impossible to determine whether or not it complies with the OSD"*, which is the bridge from poor drafting to an OSD rejection, already built. **Had the favourable reading not been sought, its refutation would not have been found either**, and B3/B13 would not have gained the weight they just did. **Three rules follow and are recorded**: state when a converse was sought and not found, rather than omitting it silently; **look for the refutation of a favourable reading in the same paragraph that states it**, because adding one side only trades one bias for its mirror; and do not blur *the worst outcome becomes less bad* with *the good outcome becomes more likely* **潰し方**: **逆側を探す規律そのものが潰し方である。** 我々にできるのは **(a) 不利な向きへ盛る圧力を明示的に測ること**、**(b) 有利な読みの反証を同じ段落で探すこと**（`BLIND-SPOTS.md` の常設次元・オーナー依頼 2026-09-13）。**この register の各 entry に `⚠ 逆側` が在るかは、まだ全数では測っていない。** |
| 141 | **Scepticism about a reviewer's provenance was allowed to filter the merits of what they said, and two fixable defects sat unfixed because of it.** On 2026-09-14 the first substantive outside review of the 1.1 draft arrived, produced by an AI and passed on by the steward. The provenance was recorded accurately and that part was right: it is not OSI feedback, not a `license-review` participant's view, and not legal review, so **B1 does not move and the packet must never present it as third-party review** — that last point matters more than usual while B14 stands. **The error was the next step.** The decomposition closed with the rule *"adopt only the dimensions our own measurement lacked"*, which conflates three separate questions: how a review may be **represented**, how far it should move **priorities**, and whether an individual point is **correct**. **The first two turn on provenance. The third does not** — a claim about a clause is settled by reading the clause. The damage was concrete: #4 (§6.3 appearing to withdraw a third party's Reservation) and #6 (§16.4's opening reading as a ban on modification) were both examined, both written up as *"defensible in substance, still misreadable"* — **and then left alone**, in a draft that **is not frozen** (Check 453 pins the 1.0 files only) | **[CLOSED]** **Corrected the same day at the owner's prompting** (*"the content and the reviewer are different things"*), and both clauses were revised on their merits. **The revisions are not the reviewer's prescriptions.** For §6.3 the proposed narrowing was **rejected** — it would drop Reservations a third party placed on the Work that the Dedicator can in fact withdraw — and the fix instead **splits two operations the clause had run together**: withdrawal, which is limited to what the Dedicator can do, and disclaiming reliance, which holds whoever made the Reservation. For §16.4, measurement showed **four of the reviewer's five requested clarifications were already in the text**, so only the missing one was added. **The standing rule is now: provenance governs attribution and weighting; merit is decided against the artifact.** **The general shape is one this dossier keeps meeting**: a discipline built to stop us believing too much became a reason to examine too little. #140 recorded the same failure pointing the other way — there, the adverse-facts habit suppressed favourable readings; here, source-scepticism suppressed valid criticism. **Both are the same mistake: letting a stance about a claim's origin stand in for testing the claim** |
| 142 | **The licence draft carried a false statement about its own state for four days, and the statement asserted that the falsehood was deliberate.** The 1.1 draft's preamble ends with a status block reading *"Still open in this draft, **deliberately**: E11 (Section 4.4, awaiting an answer from the list), E13 (the definition order), E15 (Section 8.4 has no materiality threshold) and E16 (Section 2.6 permits an opt-out that leaves no trace). **They are not oversights**; each is recorded with what it would cost."* **All four were closed.** E13 was closed on 2026-09-10, E15 on 09-11, E16 on 09-13, E11 on 09-14 — and the same preamble's own changelog described three of those fixes a few lines above, so **the document contradicted itself within one page**. It was found by adversarially auditing my own edits rather than by any gate, **and the gate that exists for this document was the thing that had the hole** **潰し方は実行済み** —— Check 468e が errata の現物から open 集合を導出して照合する。**両向きの発火を実測済みで、導入直後に作者（私）の誤りを 1 件指摘した**（*routed* は *closed* ではない）。 |

| 147 | **One clause purports to act on something a third party did, and the reader cannot tell whether it reached them.** §6.3 says that where a Reservation exists *"whether by the Dedicator or **by another person**"*, the Dedicator *"withdraws it and disclaims reliance on it, **to the fullest extent the Dedicator is able**"*. **Measured 2026-09-15: this is the only clause in ACD-1.0 that speaks to another party's act.** Every other mention of third parties binds the Dedicator's own conduct (§5.1 and §8.6 covenant not to *"authorise or assist any other person to assert"*; §8.2 is the absence of retaliation; §2.7 is a disclaimer of reach). **The objection is not ours — it is the Licensing Committee chair's, stated in a live review four days ago**: *"You're putting the burden on the potentially wronged party to limit their choices rather than on the licensor to avoid wrongful conduct ... | 148 | **We diagnosed a missing section that was not missing, wrote it, and thereby created a real duplicate.** On 2026-09-15 four documents were seen to cite `review-rules.md` §1.82; a scan for headings reported that no such section existed, so one was written. **The scan matched `### ` — three hashes — and the existing §1.82 is a `## ` heading.** The section had been there since 2026-09-13, it covered the same process-page rules, and it carried an explicit note saying the Decision-Date rule *"is not a new fact — do not mix it in"* — **which the new section then mixed in.** **The diagnosis was wrong and the fix introduced the defect.** A wider check then found the shared §1.xx numbering — split across four files — already had three collisions (§1.67, §1.68, §1.69), so a citation of "§1.68" resolves to two different sections depending on which file the reader opens. | **[CLOSED]** **Duplicate removed 2026-09-15** (the section written that day was deleted in full; the 2026-09-13 one stands). **The section created the same day in `review-precedents.md` was renumbered §1.83 → §1.88** and its two references followed. **Now machine-enforced**: Check 471 face (f) fails on any *new* collision in the shared series; the three pre-existing ones are grandfathered because renumbering them would leave existing citations pointing nowhere decidable. **Unresolved**: those three remain ambiguous, and no layer can tell which one a given citation meant. **`BLIND-SPOTS.md` already carried the rule** — *「番号つきの節を途中に挿入したら、その文書の番号が一意か・昇順かをその場で導出して確かめる」* — **and it was not followed.** **検証 2026-09-24**: `review-rules.md` の `1.82` 見出しは **1 件のみ**（重複は削除済み）。**Check 471 の face (f)** が実装に在り、**新たな衝突で落ちる。** |

Length, venue, moderation (#119 and B14, which post-dates the post), and chance all remain live, and the archive cannot separate them. **Recorded honestly**: the first scan of this question reported *"ten candidates, half unanswered, we are not alone"* — **an error in our favour**, caused by undecoded `[EXTERNAL]` subject lines splitting the PUWL thread and by counting four posts that were not pre-review requests at all. **A dossier whose whole claim is "check us" pays more for a favourable error than an unfavourable one** (`review-corpus.md` §1.90). **潰し方**: **答えるのは list の側である。** 我々にできるのは **(a) 母集団を狭く取って慰めに使わないこと**（n=3 → n=5 で広げ、結論は softening しなかった）、**(b) #215 以降は「少なくとも 3 通が moderation で拒否された」を併記すること** ——**沈黙の一部は配送されていないことによる。** |
| 150 | **The submission packet never discussed the approved licence that is closest to us on the one axis we lean on hardest.** `submission-reference.md` §2 argues the nearest instruments on *zero conditions* (0BSD) and on *patents* (Apache-2.0) and **never mentioned `CAL-1.0`**. The cause is comparing only against the set that flatters us — the same shape as *"a reference class is never just one"* in `BLIND-SPOTS.md` |
